- **Dashboard degraded-read "data may be incomplete" banner (498).** Surfaces the already-computed `LogBackendReadError` failure (previously caught-and-discarded in `dashboard/costs.py:load_runs`) as a non-blocking banner on BOTH the global cost view and the per-agent drilldown. **Data flow (maintainer ruling option B):** keeps the spec/22 `LogBackend.query()` read path; does NOT adopt the `_costs`-internal cost-summing reader / `CostReadResult` (that path is do-not-export and returns a scalar that cannot feed per-record charts). New private `_load_runs_with_degraded(...) -> tuple[list[RunRecord], bool]` returns the degraded signal; the public `load_runs` is unchanged (wraps the helper, discards the flag — callers in `activity.py`/`quality.py` need no change). `aggregate_global` OR-composes the flag across EVERY read feeding a number on the view (this-month KPIs, prior-period delta, monthly trend); `aggregate_agent` propagates the single 12-month load's flag. New defaulted field `cost_data_degraded: bool = False` on BOTH `GlobalSummary` and `AgentDashboardData` (name mirrors `CostCheckResult.cost_data_degraded` from 495 for cross-surface coherence). On BOTH dataclasses the default is the last field after all-non-defaulted predecessors, so it is CHOSEN, not forced (Python's field-ordering rule only forbids a non-defaulted field AFTER a defaulted one — a no-default field in last position would be valid). The reason for the `GlobalSummary` default is forward-compat: it is the one dashboard dataclass serialized to a JSON sidecar (via `to_json_dict` written by `render_global`), and `False` keeps a pre-498 sidecar's absent key readable per Principle 1/14. `AgentDashboardData` is HTML-only and never serialized, so its default is optional, kept only for consistency. **Intentionally boolean-only — NO `dropped_records` count:** the query() path raises before returning any records, so a record count has no honest definition here; shipping a same-named field with a different unit than `_costs.CostReadResult.dropped_records` (which counts per-line corruption) would break the very cross-surface coherence the ruling sought. The banner adds a `.degraded-banner` wrapper plus a `.pill.warn` modifier (reusing the existing `--warn` color token and `.pill` base, mirroring `.pill.fallback`'s values) — no new JS or render component. **Posture (ruling 4):** the reporting layer degrades gracefully (renders the partial total + banner) and NEVER fail-closes or crashes — only the gate layer fail-closes. **Tests:** unit tests prove `_load_runs_with_degraded`/`aggregate_*` propagate the flag, each with a per-invocation negative control and a branch-distinctive assertion on `cost_data_degraded`/banner text rather than the shared empty-render path (`feedback_layered_except_typed_branch_false_green`). **Per-CONTRIBUTOR isolation for `aggregate_global`:** because `aggregate_global` OR-merges the degraded flag from TWO independent reads of a failing backend (the this_month/last_month KPI load AND the monthly-trend load, which also reads the current month), a single current-month-degraded test cannot detect a regression that breaks EITHER OR-term alone (stripping the KPI term leaves the trend term firing — the half-broken-banner false-green). Per `feedback_false_green_test_needs_per_invocation_negative_control` ("strip EACH independent part separately; coverage % won't catch it, only the negative control does"), THREE isolation tests give the failing read DISJOINT windows/occurrences — one per independent OR sub-term: the trend term (fail an OLD month 2 back, which the KPI reads never touch), the `deg_this` sub-term (fail query 1 = this_month KPI read), and the `deg_last` sub-term (fail query 2 = last_month KPI read). Each strip was verified by hand to bite exactly ONE test (strip `or deg_this` → this_month test red, others green; strip `or deg_last` → last_month test red, others green; strip `or trend_degraded` → trend test red, others green — symbolic OR-term citations, not line numbers, which drift on any edit above them). Two integration tests assert the literal `data may be incomplete` string appears in the rendered HTML from a real degraded read (global + per-agent); two more pin the boundary cases (empty/first-run vault → no banner; all-agents-degraded → banner + crash-free all-zero summary). **`/ship` review (4 specialists + Red Team + fresh-Opus cross-family; Codex out of credits):** Testing + the coverage audit independently CONFIRMED a false-green — the original KPI isolation test bit only `deg_this`, so stripping `or deg_last` left every test green; fixed with the `deg_last` sibling (fail_on=2) + negative-control verified. Maintainability caught a backwards dataclass field-ordering comment (claimed the default was syntactically MANDATORY; it is CHOSEN — corrected in both dataclasses + this entry). Red Team flagged the unpinned empty-vault + all-degraded paths (added). Performance: no duplicate reads. Two follow-ups filed (NOT 498 regressions): 517 (pre-existing unescaped log-derived model-ID XSS in `render.py`), 518 (banner is blind to per-line log corruption — the inherited spec/22 per-line-skip boundary the option-B ruling kept; a false-negative the cost-summing reader would catch but the dashboard deliberately doesn't consume). **Spec:** spec/09's LOCKED §"Cost-read error posture (fail-closed-when-blind)" previously described 498 as an unshipped follow-up framed around the cost-summing reader. The arc build HALTED on that LOCKED normative prose; the maintainer approved the exact reword, applied in this PR — lines 300/310 now describe the banner as shipped and fed by the spec/22 `query()`/`LogBackendReadError` degraded signal (NOT the cost-summing reader, which stays `_costs`-internal, do-not-export), preserve the render-partial-rather-than-crash contract, and drop the trailing 498 open-follow-up clause so 511 stands alone. No new spec number. Closes 498.
- **Mandate SUM-helper cap-gating — resolves the 506 escalation-only-no-cap over-block AND closes a Policy-cap fail-open (512).** Cap-gates the two prior-spend SUM helpers (`_sum_prior_token_cost`, `_sum_prior_external_cost` in `judge/mandate_check.py`) so their blind-read fail-close is gated on whether the corresponding cap is active, mirroring `_project_token_cost`. Each SUM helper now takes a `cap_active` parameter; `_step7` / `_step8` (and the cap-exceeded verdict re-reads) pass the **EFFECTIVE**-cap-active flag. When `cap_active=True` a genuine read-failure raises `_MandateCostUnreadable` → BLOCK `mandate_cost_unreadable` as before; when `cap_active=False` the SUM read degrades to `$0.00` (ALLOW) instead of BLOCK. This eliminates the 506-acknowledged residual over-block where a mandate with only an escalation threshold and **no** cap would BLOCK on a SUM read failure even though the summed value gates nothing in that configuration — the escalate decision uses the projection, and the cumulative is compared only against absent caps (`feedback_fail_closed_only_where_theres_something_to_protect`). **`cap_active` reflects the EFFECTIVE cap = `MIN(mandate constraint, Policy-composed cap)`, NOT the mandate-declared cap alone:** the cap actually enforced in steps 7-8 is `MIN(mandate, Policy)` via `_min_or_other`, and `CostCaps` daily/monthly compose onto BOTH the token and external budgets. `evaluate()` derives `token_cap_active`/`external_cap_active = has_*_cap OR _has_policy_cap` (any `policy_effective_caps.daily_usd`/`monthly_usd`) and threads them through `_step7`/`_step8` (whose `cap_active` parameter replaced the misleadingly-named `has_token_cap`/`has_external_cap`) into every prior-spend read. **This closes a fail-open the first cap-gating cut introduced:** an agent running under a Policy daily/monthly cost cap whose mandate declares NO cap of its own would have degraded a blind prior-spend read to `$0.00` and ALLOWed against a live Policy budget — a never-under-report-spend violation against a real, active cap (a Policy cap IS something to protect). The genuinely-uncapped case (no mandate cap AND no Policy cap) still degrades to `$0.00`. The three `cap_active` helper defaults flip to `True` (fail-CLOSED) so an omitted flag over-blocks rather than silently fail-opens (every live caller passes it explicitly). The *never-under-report-spend* invariant is preserved because under-reporting prior spend only matters against an **active** (effective) cap. **spec/29's ` Blind-read fail-closed posture` addendum bumped v1→v2 (issues 506/512):** the helper-list sentence now states all three reads are cap-gated against the **effective** cap, and the prior Policy-only-cap "diagnostic-only, decision never wrong" corner paragraph is corrected (a Policy-only cap DOES raise `cap_active`, so the budget-step SUM fails closed against it). The 506 regression test that pinned the escalation-only-no-cap over-block as fail-SAFE is updated to assert the new degrade-to-ALLOW behavior; a new Policy-only-cap regression test (token + external) asserts the effective-cap fail-close, both with per-invocation negative controls (strip the Policy-cap inclusion / the cap → flips to ALLOW). **`/ship` adversarial (fresh-Opus cross-family, Codex out of credits) caught a latent escalation-gate fail-open; maintainer ruled fix-now:** the baseline projection `_project_token_cost` also feeds the step-9 escalation gate, so gating it on cap-presence alone meant a blind baseline read in an escalation-threshold-only mandate degraded to the conservative default and could silently **suppress** an escalation a truthful (high-baseline) read would have fired (defeating an operator's "require approval above `$X`" control). `_step7` now passes the projection a separate `projection_cap_active = token_cap_active OR has_token_escalation_threshold` so it fails closed when EITHER a cap OR an escalation threshold is the active control; the SUM still gates on the cap alone (it feeds only the cumulative-vs-cap check). spec/29 v2 reworded from "cap-gated" to "**control-gated**" — each read fails closed against exactly the controls it feeds — so the escalation-only-no-cap config is now fail-SAFE on **both** reads (SUM degrades to `$0.00`, projection fails closed `mandate_cost_unreadable`). New regression test `test_escalation_only_no_cap_blind_projection_blocks_protects_escalation` with a per-invocation negative control (strip the escalation inclusion → BLOCK flips to ALLOW); the 506 `_project_token_cost` no-cap-degrade test repurposed to a direct unit test of the now-defensive degrade primitive (unreachable via the live `_step7` path, where the projection always gates a control). **Recovery note:** the working-tree implementation was twice lost to an errant `git checkout <file>` on uncommitted work and reconstructed (once by replaying the arc-build's recorded edits, verified by the full test suite + negative-control bite; once by re-applying the just-made edits) — lesson saved. Closes 512.
- **Mandate spend-gate fail-closed-on-blind-read — completes the 497 read-side posture + spec/29 versioned normative addendum (506).** Flips the mandate spend-gate's prior-spend read helpers (`judge/mandate_check.py`) from the long-standing `except Exception → return 0.0 (optimistic fallback)` **fail-OPEN** to a **fail-CLOSED** BLOCK. 497 shipped an INTERIM guard for the outstanding-*reservation* read only; 506 completes the *prior-committed-spend* read surface. **New private `_MandateCostUnreadable` sentinel** raised by the read helpers and caught by a single top-level handler in `evaluate()` that returns BLOCK reason `mandate_cost_unreadable` — a **new THIRD reason**, distinct from and NOT retiring the two 497 reasons (`mandate_token_reservations_unreadable` / `mandate_external_reservations_unreadable`; retiring forever-stable audit tokens would be a Principle 14 audit-log break). The catch covers the **full read surface** — steps 7-8 budget sums AND the step-9 cap-exceeded verdict re-reads — fixing the 497 P2 concise-vs-detailed "steps 7-8 only" scope mismatch. **NARROW catch (maintainer ruling, deviates from the issue's literal "match the broad `_sum_via_backend` two-branch"):** the helpers catch only `_NARROW_READ_FAILURE = (LogBackendReadError, OSError, sqlite3.DatabaseError)`; code defects (`KeyError`/`TypeError`/`AttributeError`) PROPAGATE unchanged rather than being relabelled a quiet fleet-wide BLOCK with a falsified audit reason (Principle 5/8 — the verdict `mandate_cost_unreadable` is a claim about the world, not a code bug). `psycopg.Error` is deliberately absent — a conforming `PostgresLogBackend` wraps it into `LogBackendReadError` before this layer (`logs/postgres.py`), so the typed member already covers it; adding it would force a hard `import psycopg` that breaks non-Postgres installs. **`_project_token_cost` is cap-gated (maintainer ruling, deviates from "flip all four"):** it fails closed only when a token/daily/monthly cap is active; with no cap it KEEPS its conservative degrade-to-default ($0.10 / `expected_cost_per_call_usd`) — its blind-read fallback *over*-projects (not a fail-open like the SUM helpers' blind-read-as-`$0`), so failing closed where there is no cap would re-introduce the 495 spurious-block regression (`feedback_fail_closed_only_where_theres_something_to_protect`). The two SUM helpers flipped to fail-closed here (subsequently cap-gated in 512, above); the outstanding-reservation reads flip unconditionally. **spec/29 (LOCKED at 124 PR4) gains a versioned normative ` Blind-read fail-closed posture` subsection** adjacent to §"Crash recovery for reservations", reworded to the full read surface and reconciling read-side fail-closed with write-side crash-recovery over-report as two faces of one *never-under-report-spend* invariant; delivered **OUTSIDE** the 8-MUST Implementer-Contract tally (the 497 spec/22 + 483 spec/41 versioned-addendum precedent). All `spec/29 line NNN` citations in `mandate_check.py` + `mandate/filesystem.py` converted to section-name citations (line citations have repeatedly drifted in this file). **`500` named seam (maintainer `best_not_cheapest` ruling), ZERO code:** the spec/29 subsection + a spec/22 cross-ref name where a future real-authz `LogBackend` plugs in a tamper-evidence MUST (append-only/signed/hash-chained cost records, so "unverifiable" subsumes "tampered") — no speculative capability flag. spec/22's forward-reference updated to past tense (506 shipped). **Conformance + regression tests** (`tests/test_mandate_check.py`, new `TestMandateCheckCostUnreadable`): per-helper × per-except-branch matrix with **per-invocation negative controls** and branch-distinctive log-line asserts (`feedback_layered_except_typed_branch_false_green` + `feedback_false_green_test_needs_per_invocation_negative_control`) — typed-blind-read BLOCK, narrow-family-member BLOCK (OSError/sqlite3), code-defect-PROPAGATES (the narrow-catch guarantee, `pytest.raises`), empty-history-still-ALLOW boundary, `_project_token_cost` no-cap-degrade vs cap-BLOCK split, and separate token-side + external-side cap-exceeded re-read controls. **`/ship` review pass (the arc build halted at the LOCKED-spec gate before its adversarial rounds, so the review army was the first adversarial pass — 3 Sonnet specialists + a fresh-Opus cross-family skeptic, Codex out of credits):** the security specialist + adversarial both confirmed the fail-closed property holds (no blind-read path reaches ALLOW; `_MandateCostUnreadable` cannot escape `evaluate()` — it is not a `JudgeError`, and the only production caller catches `JudgeError` only). Fixed: a **P1 test-flakiness** root cause (fixed-length `query.side_effect` lists colliding with `compute_outstanding`'s variable query count) replaced with a shape-keyed, per-occurrence dispatcher (`_query_blind_on`, drift-immune; 10× determinism loop green); a **P2 false-green** where the `with_cap` test's `"cap active"` log assertion was satisfied by the exception text rather than the branch label (exception message reworded; negative-control verified by blanking the label); a **P2 missing negative control** for the token-side cap-exceeded re-read (no test — added, plus the docstring on the external-side test corrected); and **3 surviving `spec/29 line NNN` citations** (one stale after the subsection line-shift) converted to section names. Both reviewers independently flagged an **escalation-only-no-cap SUM over-block** (the SUM helpers fail closed even when no cap gates their value) — ruled fail-SAFE + spec-consistent, documented in the spec/29 addendum, pinned by a regression test, and filed as **512** (cap-gate the SUM helpers too — since RESOLVED, see the 512 bullet above). Follow-ups: **511** (mandate startup recovery-scan raise-vs-skip posture — the live-gate-vs-recovery-scan asymmetry named in the addendum; NOT 498, which is the dashboard banner), **512** (SUM-helper cap-gating refinement). Separate from 500 (tamper-evidence → real-authz backend) and 497 (merged). Closes 506.
- **LogBackend read-failure contract — typed `LogBackendReadError` + spec/22 versioned normative addendum (497 PR1).** Promotes the 495 cost-read "fail-closed when blind" posture into a NORMATIVE, conformance-paired LogBackend contract so every reference backend (and every future impl) distinguishes the absent/empty state from a genuine unrecoverable read failure. **New typed exception** `LogBackendReadError(AtomicAgentsError)` in `atomic_agents/exceptions.py`, exported from `atomic_agents/__init__.py` + `__all__`. **All three reference backends** (`logs/filesystem.py`, `logs/sqlite.py`, `logs/postgres.py`) now wrap native unrecoverable-read failures from `query()` / `tail()` / `aggregate()` into `LogBackendReadError` (filesystem `OSError`; SQLite `sqlite3.DatabaseError`/`OperationalError` at connect/execute; Postgres `psycopg.Error` surviving the one-shot reconnect — all narrow catches, with exception chaining via `raise ... from exc`). `stats()` is **EXEMPT** (keeps its locked racy-diagnostic "callers MUST NOT use for control decisions" contract). **Empty/absent/missing state still returns `[]`** (MUST NOT raise) — the filesystem boundary is fine-grained: directory-level ENOENT (dir vanished after `.exists()`, TOCTOU) → `[]`; month-dir ENOENT mid-walk → skip; per-file ENOENT (file vanished between listing and open) → skip; per-file non-ENOENT `OSError` (EIO/EACCES on a listed-but-unreadable day file) → raise (fail closed). **Cost reader** (`_costs._sum_via_backend`) gets a **layered catch**: `except LogBackendReadError` first (clean "genuine unrecoverable read failure" log) then the broad `except Exception` backstop, both returning `CostReadResult(total_usd=0.0, degraded=True)` — the typed catch sharpens the signal; the broad catch is the unconditional fail-closed backstop for non-conforming custom backends. **Mandate spend-gate INTERIM guard:** `judge/mandate_check.py` step-7 (token budget) + step-8 (external budget) catch `LogBackendReadError` BEFORE their broad `except` and BLOCK (`mandate_token_reservations_unreadable` / `mandate_external_reservations_unreadable`) so the outstanding-reservation read fails CLOSED rather than widening the pre-existing fail-OPEN to a total undercount. This is a maintainer-ruled MINIMAL interim guard — the FULL mandate posture (the prior-cost-sum read sites, the Postgres-config `ValueError` reconnect path, a unified `BLOCK mandate_cost_unreadable` across all read methods, the spec/29 amendment, and a typed-return replacement for the dict-key fail-closed signal) stays **506**. **Reporting/analysis consumers degrade gracefully** (not control gates): `dashboard/costs.py` `load_runs` → `[]`, `dashboard/quality.py` `_count_provenance` → `(0, 0)`, `dream.py` `_read_log_lines` → proceed with empty log signal (the dream log read runs before the cost gate, so a degrade cannot leak uncosted spend; airtight in both capped and uncapped cases per the 495 "fail-closed only where there is something to protect" lesson). **spec/22 gets a versioned normative read-failure addendum** ("Cost-read / blind-read read-failure posture") that is fully normative + conformance-paired but sits **OUTSIDE** the 8-MUST queryable-backend Implementer Contract count (the spec/41 483 versioned-addendum precedent) — every existing "8-MUST" cross-reference stays valid (verified: no `8-MUST` cross-ref exists in README / CLAUDE.md / protocols-shipped.md). spec/09's cost-read posture table + narrative updated to the split posture (outstanding-reservation read now fail-CLOSED via the 497 guard; prior-cost-sum sites remain fail-OPEN, 506). **Conformance + impl tests:** per-backend `break_read()` injection proving each backend raises `LogBackendReadError` on a forced read failure + a reader-seam test proving `_sum_via_backend` fails closed; filesystem empty-vs-failure errno-boundary tests (6 for `query()` + 2 `tail()` skip-branch); a Postgres connect-time read-error wrap test for `tail()`/`aggregate()`; 2 mandate fail-closed regression tests (negative-control verified — the proposal is ALLOWED with the guard stripped). **`/ship` review pass:** the Testing specialist caught a **false-green** in the two `_sum_via_backend` "typed branch fires" tests — `degraded=True` alone does not prove the typed handler ran because the broad `except Exception` also catches `LogBackendReadError` and returns the same result; fixed by asserting the typed branch's distinctive log line ("genuine unrecoverable read failure") via `caplog`, then **negative-control-verified** (stripping the typed handler makes both tests fail because the broad backstop logs "raised unexpected" instead, Principle 12). Red-team + Testing flagged missing `tail()` skip-branch coverage and missing `__cause__` chaining asserts on the conformance `tail()`/`aggregate()` raise tests — both added. A Postgres test docstring that over-claimed it exercised the post-connect `_run_with_reconnect` wrap (it hits the connect-time `_get_conn_for_read` wrap) was corrected (Principle 13). The fresh-Opus cross-family adversarial (Codex out of credits) caught this spec/22 test census drift (231 → 240) and confirmed the empty-vs-failure boundary, the layered catch ordering, exception chaining, and the LOCKED-spec 8-MUST integrity by empirical negative control. Follow-ups filed inline: **507** (DRY: extract the filesystem read-failure OSError/ENOENT guard helpers + dedup the `_get_conn_for_read` docstrings), **508** (align Postgres read-error messages with the file's DSN-redaction discipline — no active leak, the Security specialist verified, a consistency item), and a **506** comment for the typed-return mandate-signal cleanup. `LogBackendReadError` read failures are separate from 500 (tamper-evident cost accounting — that waits for a real-authz backend). Closes 497.
- **Goal coordinator backend-universe alignment (496 PR1).** `dispatch_sub_goal_as_outcome()` (`atomic_agents/goal/coordinator.py`) now reads the gate agent's public `log_backend`, `policy_backend`, and `profile_backend` attributes and threads them as keyword args into the `OutcomeRunner(...)` construction, so the runner's internal `AtomicAgent` reads cost/log data and spends through the SAME backend instances the pre-dispatch cost gate checked. Previously the runner resolved its own default (filesystem) backends, so a programmatic caller who pinned a custom `LogBackend`/`PolicyBackend`/`AgentProfileBackend` on the gate agent (e.g. SQLite or Postgres) would have the gate read one store and the run spend through another — making the gate's allow/deny decision based on the wrong data. **Backward-compatible:** the default-filesystem CLI path (`GoalManager.dispatch_as_outcome()` → `python -m atomic_agents.goal dispatch-outcome`) is byte-identical (the gate agent is built with default backends, so all three forwarded attrs are filesystem defaults); only the custom-backend programmatic caller changes. `outcome_backend` is intentionally not threaded (the runner owns its outcome write-path topology). **Scope caveat:** `mandate_backend` is not yet threaded, so a custom mandate pinned on the gate agent can still produce a divergent (looser) cap in the runner — tracked in 503. Sibling shim follow-up 502 tracks threading the same three backends through `GoalManager.dispatch_as_outcome()` if it ever gains custom-backend construction. spec/41 §"Goal-outcome composition" gains a non-normative universe-alignment note (NOT an 11th MUST). Tests: a coordinator boundary-1 instance-identity test + a custom-backend forwarding test (`test_goal_coordinator.py`) and a new boundary-2 forwarding test (`test_outcome.py::test_outcome_runner_forwards_backends_to_internal_agent`) that asserts `OutcomeRunner.run()` constructs its internal `AtomicAgent` with the threaded log/policy/profile backends — closing the previously code-read-only gap in the end-to-end chain.
- **Cost-read fail-closed posture (internal refactor, 495 PR1).** `sum_cost_for_period` and `_sum_via_backend` now return a `CostReadResult(total_usd, degraded, dropped_records)` dataclass instead of a bare `float`. `CostCheckResult` gains `cost_data_degraded: bool = False` (public, backward-compatible — existing `CostCheckResult(allow=True)` callers unchanged). Two-tier fail-closed logic: whole-file OSError on the current-day log, an unreadable month directory, a raw `Path.exists()` OSError on the current-day probe, or any backend exception → `degraded=True` / fail-closed (closes the prior fail-OPEN where `Path.glob()` swallowed a scandir `EACCES` and reported `$0` spent). Per-line JSON/float corruption below 50% of non-empty lines (per-file threshold) → skip + `logger.warning` (deduped by path+reason per process) + `degraded=True`; above 50% → fail-closed. Blindness is handled per-file by recency: a **historical** file that is unreadable or majority-corrupt is skipped (+`degraded`, partial month total), and only the **current-day** guardrail file fails the whole read closed — a single garbage old daily log cannot brick the gate for the rest of the month. All gate sites (`_check_cost_guardrails`, `_check_batch_reservation`, delegation headroom, `dream._check_cap`) gate on `cost_guardrails_enabled` BEFORE reading cost (a guardrails-disabled agent is never fail-closed by a degraded read), then map `degraded=True` → fail-closed / over-cap; the degraded `_check_cost_guardrails` path returns `action="skip"` (never `fallback` with an unset model — a fallback on a blind read would still spend on a cheaper LLM, a fail-open). The coordinator `coordinator_dispatch_rejected` event carries a structured `cost_data_degraded` field unconditionally; the two `call()` cost-skip JSONL records (pre-loop + mid-loop) add `cost_data_degraded=true` only on the blind-read path (absent on a normal cap hit) so audit can distinguish a real cap hit from a blind-read fail-close. The degraded fail-close fires only when there is a budget to enforce — an own daily/monthly cap (model.md or Policy) OR a parent coordinator tree-cap (`parent_remaining_headroom_usd`). An **uncapped** (warnings-only) agent with no caps and no parent tree-cap is NOT blocked by a blind read: with no cap to bypass it always proceeds even with perfect data, so blocking would be a spurious refusal with zero safety benefit (the allowed result still carries `cost_data_degraded=True` for audit honesty). This uncapped-skip applies uniformly across all four gate sites including `dream._check_cap`. The backend-routed path (`_sum_via_backend`, used for non-filesystem backends on **both** the `today` and `this_month` reads) fail-closes on any `query()` exception or when >50% of cost-bearing records have an un-coerced/non-numeric `cost_usd`; that per-record drop path is a defensive belt only — every shipped backend pre-coerces `cost_usd` to `float | None` (typed DB columns or `RunRecord.from_dict`), so a non-numeric value is coerced to `None` and skipped upstream rather than dropped, and a numeric string is summed like the filesystem `float()` coercion. A JSON boolean `cost_usd` (e.g. `true`) is treated as corruption (dropped/degraded) on **both** the filesystem and backend paths rather than silently coerced to `$1.00` via `float(True)` (`bool` is an `int` subclass) — the two cost-read paths are symmetric on every malformed value. An empty/whitespace-only current-day file is treated as no-cost (`degraded=False`), identical to an absent file — NOT as blind: the log writer's `open("a")` creates the file before the first write, so a concurrent reader hits a legitimate 0-byte window on every first append of the day and must not fail-close on it. The month-directory enumeration uses a single `os.scandir` handle (closing the prior probe-then-reglob TOCTOU window where a late `EACCES` could fail-OPEN). The dashboard cost aggregation is unaffected: it reads through the spec/22 `LogBackend.query()` path (partial-line tolerant, unchanged). spec/09 amended with a descriptive cost-read error posture note. `CostReadResult` is an internal type — not in `__init__.__all__`. **`/ship` review pass:** the build's adversarial review flagged two spurious-block regressions, both fixed here — (1) an empty/whitespace current-day file fail-closed (it conflated "no content yet" with "blind"; the `open("a")` append race makes it a normal-path false block — now treated as no-cost); (2) the degraded fail-close fired for uncapped/warnings-only agents at every gate site (`_check_cost_guardrails`, `_check_batch_reservation`, delegation headroom, `dream._check_cap`) where there is no cap to bypass — now gated on an effective own cap or parent tree-cap. Also: the backend bool-`cost_usd` guard added for fs/backend parity, and ~800 lines of unrelated ruff reflow (pre-existing non-conformance the formatter hook pulled into incidentally-touched test files + `types.py`) reverted so the diff shows only functional changes. Regression tests added for each (empty-file, uncapped-skip across all four gates, backend-bool). Cross-family review (Grok) surfaced that the empty-file→no-cost rule means truncating/deleting the cost log resets apparent spend — a real integrity gap, but pre-existing + unchanged by 495 and within the T15 vault-writer-trusted boundary (same actor can forge `$0` records or edit `model.md` caps); documented in spec/09 §"Trust-boundary note" and filed as 500 (tamper-evident cost accounting → real-authz backend). Follow-up 497 filed to evaluate a LogBackend Implementer Contract MUST once SQLite/Postgres has dogfooded read-error semantics; follow-up 498 filed for a degraded-aware "data may be incomplete" dashboard banner if/when a reporting consumer adopts this reader.

- **GoalBackend clock injection + GoalManager thin shim + agent_root resolution + spec/41 addendum** ([483](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/483), [#485](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/485), [#486](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/486) — PR1, goal-adoption cleanup bundle). **Injectable clock (`when: date | None = None`)** added to `GoalBackend.apply_transition()` (controls `# History` prose date prefix only; JSONL `ts` is caller-supplied and unchanged) and `GoalBackend.archive_goal()` (controls ALL four date-stamped fields in one archive: slug prefix, `archived_at`, `last_progress_check`, ` History` prose). Fixed by single `today` resolution before the file lock. **GoalManager thin shim (483)** — `GoalManager.archive()` replaced the ~60-line in-place archival duplicate (its own frontmatter parsing, slug construction, separate `_append_history("goal archived")`) with a one-method delegation: `slug = self.goal_backend.archive_goal(self.agent_name, reason=reason, when=self.today)`, then reconstructs `Path` from slug + nulls `self._goal`. **Double history prose closed:** the old `GoalManager.archive()` called `self._append_history("goal archived")` AND `FilesystemGoalBackend` wrote its own prose, producing two "goal archived" entries in ` History`. Removed the `GoalManager._append_history` call from the archive/abandon path; backend owns the prose exclusively. **GoalManager active-goal precheck preserved (no false-success delta):** `GoalManager.archive()` keeps the `if not self.has_goal(): raise AtomicAgentsError("No active goal to archive")` precheck. The thin shim must NOT let backend MUST 9 idempotency (return the most-recently-modified archive slug when goal.md is absent) leak to the GoalManager public boundary — at that boundary it produced a false success: a second `abandon()` returned a stale prior-archive `Path`, exit 0, with the operator's `--reason` silently discarded. MUST 9 retry-after-unlink idempotency stays intact for DIRECT `backend.archive_goal()` crash-retry callers (the legitimate consumer); the GoalManager contract (raise when nothing is active) is unchanged from pre-483, so there is no public-API behavior delta. The `goal abandon` CLI handler now catches that raise and exits non-zero with an honest `Error:` message (Principle 5 audit honesty, Principle 14 backward-compat). Guarded by `test_archive_on_already_archived_agent_raises_not_stale_return` (GoalManager API + CLI). **agent_root resolution (486)** — `GoalManager.__init__` now resolves BOTH `self.agents_root` (`Path(agents_root or get_agents_root()).resolve()`) and `self.agent_root` (`(self.agents_root / agent_name).resolve()`) at construction time. strengthened to a two-level within-vault symlink topology that fails if either resolve is removed (verified by negative control, Principle 12). Cross-family Codex (load-bearing on this path/containment surface — the 492 lesson) caught that the `486` code comment OVERCLAIMED "escaping symlinks are caught by the backend" — they are not: `resolve()` follows an agent-directory symlink that escapes the vault and the backend then anchors containment on the escaped target. Per the maintainer's ruling (consistent with TENSIONS T15 / spec/44: vault-writer is in-scope-trusted, within-perimeter planting out of scope), the comment + spec/41 addendum were corrected to document the boundary honestly (Principle 13) rather than add a `is_relative_to` guard that would contradict T15 and break legitimate cross-volume agent storage. The raw `.resolve()` in `GoalManager.__init__` was wrapped to fold `OSError`/`RuntimeError` (symlink loop) into `PathTraversalError` so a malformed vault path fails closed instead of crashing construction (journal-backend 427 precedent), guarded by `test_goalmanager_resolve_failure_fails_closed`. The `archive()` docstring now documents the best-effort-precheck-outside-lock concurrency note and the injected-backend same-root contract (both narrow, single-session COARSE-ROUTE scope; not shipped-CLI-path issues). Follow-up 494 (tighten `has_goal()` predicate to `is_file()`) filed inline. Test suite: **4,465 collected** (4,393 passed / 72 skipped; net +11 from prior 4,454 — conformance 56→60 (TEST 56–59), golden 30→35, filesystem 14→16, 2026-06-13; +1 golden in 494 for 36 total).

- **Goal-outcome COORDINATOR + fail-closed cost gate + CAS guard + spec/41 LOCKED** ([448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR3 of 3 — arc-closer). Ships `atomic_agents/goal/coordinator.py` — `dispatch_sub_goal_as_outcome(agent, goal_manager, sub_goal_id, rubric, ...)` is a thin free function (NOT a `GoalBackend` method) that composes `GoalBackend` + `OutcomeRunner` with the full pre-dispatch pipeline: **(1) validation** (sub-goal must be `pending` or `in_progress`; `blocked_by` if set must reference a `complete` sub-goal); **(2) fail-closed cost gate** — `result = agent._check_cost_guardrails(critical=False)`, read `result.allow` as a dataclass attribute (NOT a 2-tuple, NOT inside a try/except — Principle #4, closes the 425 fail-open bug); on not-allow: `append_history_event(coordinator_dispatch_rejected, reason)` FIRST (audit-before-raise ordering, MUST 6), then raise `CostGuardrailBlocked` — no `apply_transition` called, sub-goal stays `pending`; **(3) pre-transition (if pending)** — `apply_transition(to_status='in_progress', event='sub_goal_outcome_started')`, lock released after write; in-memory `goal_manager._goal` updated to the returned `Goal` so callers inspecting the in-memory state during the run see `in_progress`; **(4) run** — `OutcomeRunner(...).run(...)`, no goal lock held during LLM calls; **(5) terminal transition** — `apply_transition(to_status=<mapped>, expected_from_status='in_progress', history_event={"event": "sub_goal_outcome_dispatched", outcome_run_id, terminal_state, applied_status, iterations, total_cost_usd, ts})`. Terminal-state mapping: `satisfied→complete` (fields=`{completed: today}`), `max_iterations_reached→blocked` (fields=`{blocked_by: None}`), `failed→blocked` (fields=`{blocked_by: None}`), `interrupted→in_progress` (stays; CAS passes; audit event lands). **`dispatch_as_outcome()` refactored to a thin shim** that constructs a real `AtomicAgent` (keyword args `name`/`agents_root`/`goal_backend`, OUTSIDE any try/except — a construction failure propagates, fail-closed) and passes it to the coordinator, so the fail-closed cost gate is **live on the CLI path** (`dispatch-outcome`) and consults the same budget universe (model.md caps) the `OutcomeRunner` will spend — `CostGuardrailBlocked` propagates to the CLI as exit 3 BEFORE the run + construction overhead is paid (Principle 4 "refuse before paying overhead"). Under the lock, after `load_goal()`, before write: if `expected_from_status is not None and sg.status != expected_from_status` → raise `GoalConcurrentModification` (new exception in `exceptions.py`, exported from `atomic_agents`) with NO write and NO JSONL append. - **GoalBackend write-path ADOPTION + `AtomicAgent.goal_backend` live-wiring + archive data-loss fix** ([448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR1 of 3 — Goal write-path adoption, COARSE-ROUTE). Routes `GoalManager`'s three persistence paths through the `GoalBackend` Protocol: `load()` → `backend.load_goal()`, `save()` → `backend.save_goal()`, `_append_goal_history_jsonl()` → `backend.append_history_event()`. Pure in-memory computation (`mark_*` mutators, `evaluate_completion`, `next_sub_goal`, cycle detection) stays above the Protocol per Principle #2/3. `apply_transition()` is RESERVED for 448 PR3 (coordinator + cost gate) — routing dispatch through it this PR would break golden tests because `apply_transition` uses `date.today()` vs. GoalManager's injectable `self.today` clock. **`AtomicAgent` now exposes `self.goal_backend`** as a live-wired public attribute + `goal_backend: GoalBackend | None = None` constructor kwarg (kwarg-wins-over-env, default `get_default_goal_backend(agent_root)` — exact mirror of `journal_backend`). `GoalManager.__init__` gains matching `goal_backend: GoalBackend | None = None` kwarg with the same precedence. `GoalBackend` imported at module level in `agent.py` so the type annotation resolves under `typing.get_type_hints()` (mirrors `JournalBackend` pattern). **INTENTIONAL data-loss fix (A3 ruling):** `GoalManager.archive()` hand-rolled a frontmatter dict that silently dropped `deadline`, `parent_goal`, `related_atomic_notes`, `related_decisions`, `related_canon_pages` — verified bug (Principle 12). This is **byte-identical for any valid goal** (validation is read-only; every `mark_*`/`add_sub_goal` mutator produces valid state) — it only changes the failure mode on already-invalid state from "write invalid bytes" to "raise", closing the write-time/read-time validation asymmetry MUST 5 requires of EVERY write path (`save_goal` AND `apply_transition`). - **OutcomeBackend write-path ADOPTION + `OutcomeRunner.outcome_backend` kwarg + spec/42 LOCKED** ([448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR2 of 3 — Outcome write-path adoption, COARSE-ROUTE). Agent ARTIFACT files still go to `output_dir` — only the audit envelope relocates. DEFAULT case (output_dir == canonical) is **byte-identical-location**. Guarded by `tests/test_outcome_adoption_golden.py` (4 tests: default-output_dir routing proof + spy asserts `write_result` called exactly once with `(agent_name, run_id, result)`; custom-output_dir relocation — also asserts the agent artifact file STAYS in the custom dir; kwarg-wins-over-env — sets `ATOMIC_AGENTS_OUTCOME_BACKEND` and proves the injected kwarg backend wins; error-propagation — a backend whose `write_result` raises makes `run()` propagate, pinning the no-catch discipline). **NO try/except around the routed write** (no split-brain / fail-open — spec/42 MUST 9 write-once and Principle #5 audit trail). `AtomicAgent.outcome_backend` remains the per-agent HANDLE for operator inspection and the future PR3 coordinator — it is NOT the write path. `AtomicAgent` does NOT construct or feed an OutcomeRunner; the two instances resolve independently from the same `agent_root`. Two-instance shape documented in comment at write site for the PR3 author. **`get_default_outcome_backend` docstring updated** (removed stale "deferred to 448" wording). **`agent.py` comment block updated** from scaffolding-only to LIVE-WIRED shape (mirrors the `journal_backend`/`goal_backend` LIVE-WIRED blocks at lines 656–692). **spec/42 LOCKED** (drift audit passed: all 9 MUSTs covered by conformance suite, no inline TODOs in spec, scaffolding-only language removed, "deferred to post-448" paragraph removed, line 262 updated with A2 nuance — OutcomeRunner write path active; AtomicAgent.outcome_backend the coordinator handle, not the write path). - **`QueueBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemQueueBackend` reference impl + DRAFT spec/44** ([428](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/428) — the seventeenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 1, SCAFFOLDING-ONLY). Carves the cascade work-queue cluster (`claim_next_queued`, `release_claim`, `move_to_dead_letter`, `recover_stale_claims`, `renew_lease`, `_sidecar_path`, `_write_sidecar`) from `atomic_agents/_cascade.py` into a swappable `QueueBackend` Protocol. Zero internal runtime callers wired this PR — the `_cascade.py` free-function API is preserved via a thin non-deprecated shim; POSIX-rename semantics and on-disk layout are byte-identical to the pre-carve codebase. New `atomic_agents/queue/` package: `backend.py` (`runtime_checkable QueueBackend` Protocol with `claim_next` / `release` / `move_to_dead_letter` / `renew_lease` / `list_claimed` / `export` / `capabilities`), `types.py` (abstract `QueueItem` Protocol type + `FilesystemQueueItem` subtype carrying `path` + frozen `QueueCapabilities` + `QueueExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemQueueBackend` reference impl), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_queue_backend` / `get_queue_backend` / `list_queue_backends` / `unregister_queue_backend` + `get_default_queue_backend(project_root)` factory). `FilesystemQueueBackend` is **project-scoped** (scope token is `project_root`, NOT `agent_root` — the one project-scoped backend in the v1.5 wave, matching spec/06 where the queue is a shared project resource). `claim_next` atomicity is the queued→claimed POSIX rename (one winner under a concurrent race; the loser gets `FileNotFoundError` and tries the next candidate). The `.lease.json` sidecar write is best-effort; `recover_stale_claims()` falls back to the work file's mtime for torn/legacy sidecars. `QueueItem` carries NO `path` field (that filesystem detail lives on `FilesystemQueueItem`) so future Redis/SQS/DB backends don't fake a path. Symlink containment mirrors the journal/outcome siblings: resolve to CHECK `is_relative_to(project_root)`, then return the UNRESOLVED path for file ops so `item.path` stays byte-identical to the pre-carve cron API. `export()` re-asserts containment on every durable leaf; export embeds DURABLE subset only (`queued/` + `done/` + `dead-letter/`) — in-flight `claimed/` and `.lease.json` sidecars are structurally excluded (runtime-bound/ephemeral, mirrors `LockExport` precedent). `QueueExport` re-exported from `atomic_agents.export` package root; `FilesystemQueueBackend` registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py` + `tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). `recover_stale_claims` is a shared free-function built only from Protocol calls (`list_claimed` + reclaim) so every backend runs the same recovery with no drift. Operator override: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_QUEUE_BACKEND` env var (default `'filesystem'`). Doctor: `check_queue_backend(agent_root)` derives `project_root` via `detect_cascade()`, validates the configured backend resolves, and WARNs (not FAILs) when `single_host_only=True` with `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MULTI_HOST=true`. Additive `role` key in `.lease.json` sidecar (ignored by legacy readers) enables `list_claimed(role=...)` filtering. 12 normative MUSTs in spec/44 Implementer Contract (base-8 PersonaBackend pattern + 4 queue-unique axes: atomic-claim-via-rename / no-double-claim-under-race; lease-expiry-recovery correctness; dead-letter terminal-transition; `single_host_only` capability honesty). Conformance suite: `tests/test_queue_backend_conformance.py` (claim-race tests + doctor + export + import-equivalence) + `tests/test_queue_filesystem.py` (POSIX-rename + `.lease.json` sidecar + legacy-mtime-fallback specifics). **Closes TENSIONS T4** (queue-claim atomicity locked to POSIX rename): with the Protocol carved out, a Redis/SQS/DB backend providing cross-host atomicity can drop in without forking the claim logic, lifting the single-host constraint for multi-host deployments. Test suite: **4,387 collected** (4,315 passed / 72 skipped; net +135 from conformance + export harness + filesystem-specific tests + the `/ship` perimeter-containment security regression suite — see # Security, 2026-06-12).

- **`JournalBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemJournalBackend` reference impl + ADOPT-NOW wiring (bundle.py / agent.py / dream.py) + DRAFT spec/43** ([427](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/427) — the sixteenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 1). Abstracts the three hand-synced `rglob` journal-read blocks that were scattered across `bundle.py`, `agent.py`, and `dream.py` behind a single `JournalBackend` Protocol. **ADOPT-NOW ruling:** all three read sites are wired in the same PR as the Protocol definition (unlike Goal/Outcome which were SCAFFOLDING-ONLY). The motivation: the three sites are distributed duplicates with no single flat module to carve — leaving them unwired would re-create the maintenance burden the Protocol eliminates. New `atomic_agents/journal/` package: `backend.py` (`runtime_checkable JournalBackend` Protocol with `append_entry` / `query_by_date` / `list_entries` / `export` / `export_all` / `capabilities`), `types.py` (canonical **frozen** `JournalEntry` + `JournalCapabilities` + `JournalExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass — **frozen is the deliberate INVERSE of goal/outcome's mutable exception**: journal entries are append-only immutable episodes, not state-machine objects; the correct analog is `LogEntry`, not `Goal`), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemJournalBackend` reference impl), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_journal_backend` / `get_journal_backend` / `list_journal_backends` / `unregister_journal_backend` + `get_default_journal_backend(agent_root)` factory + `_redact_for_error_message` credential-safety helper). `JournalCorrupted` exception added to `atomic_agents.exceptions` (mirrors `GoalCorrupted`/`OutcomeCorrupted`). **`JournalEntry.path` carries the caller's UN-resolved path shape** (NOT resolved/absolutized — relative if `agent_root` was relative, symlinked if it was symlinked) to stay byte-identical with the legacy `rglob` callers (resolving it would shift the bundle backtick render line + the `_source_paths`/`_staleness_paths` set that drives cascade `.lease.json` freshness) — storing `path.name` was a latent `dream.py:294` subdir-loss bug (month subdir `2026-06/` dropped) that this backend fixes; callers that need the filename string use `entry.path.name` explicitly; `export()` handles relativization. **Append atomicity (MUST 9):** exclusive `fcntl.flock` on a `.journal.lock` sidecar at `agent_root` level serializes the entire read-modify-append sequence (read current file → append text → `atomic_write`) — preventing the lost-update race where two concurrent callers read stale content and the second write clobbers the first. The `.journal.lock` sidecar lives at agent_root level (NOT inside `journal/`); `rglob('*.md')` never matches it; doctor MUST NOT treat it as corruption. **Date-query correctness (MUST 10):** `query_by_date(start, end)` returns exactly the entries in `[start, end]` plus all entries whose filename stem cannot be parsed as an ISO date (include-on-unparse fallback — matches legacy `dream._read_journal_entries`; the one deliberate divergence is that legacy caught only `OSError` and would PROPAGATE a `UnicodeDecodeError` (crashing consolidation), whereas `query_by_date` also skips non-UTF-8 files — strictly safer than legacy). **`list_entries()` sort order:** sort by full `Path` descending (NOT by `JournalEntry.date`) — path-based sort preserves the lexicographic ordering that all three legacy `rglob` callers relied on; sorting on `date` alone swaps positions at month boundaries, silently breaking bundle's staleness hash. **Symlink containment:** `_journal_dir()` raises `PathTraversalError` when `journal/` resolves outside `agent_root` (mirrors `FilesystemOutcomeBackend._runs_root()` pattern; load-bearing per MEMORY.md `feedback_cross_model_catches_same_family_blind_spots` — cross-family Codex review confirmed this HIGH-severity gap). Individual symlinked `.md` ENTRY files (symlink-to-file resolving under `agent_root`) are NOT filtered — they are selected and read through exactly as the three legacy `rglob` callers did (`is_file()`/`_safe_read_text`/`read_text` all follow symlinks); per-entry filtering would shift the newest-N selection (the byte-identity regression the ADOPT-NOW ruling forbids). Only the symlinked `journal/` DIRECTORY escape is refused. **STORAGE-ONLY boundary:** the backend owns the data layer; formatting STAYS at each call site. The divergence between bundle format (WITH backtick path line `\`{entry.path}\``) and agent format (WITHOUT) is **LOAD-BEARING** — byte-identity golden tests in the conformance suite (TESTS 44–45) freeze both render formats. Do NOT unify them. **Bundle / agent / dream call-site changes:** `bundle._render_journal_breakpoint()` and `bundle._source_paths()` / `bundle._staleness_paths()` replace their `rglob` blocks with `JournalBackend.list_entries()`; `agent._load_recent_journal()` replaces its `rglob` block with `list_entries()`; `dream._run_pipeline()` and `dream.start()` replace `_read_journal_entries()` with `query_by_date()` + a dict-adapter (`{"filename": entry.path.name, "text": entry.text}`) that preserves all 13+ downstream callers in `dream.py` unchanged. `AtomicAgent.__init__` gains `journal_backend: JournalBackend | None = None` kwarg + `self.journal_backend` attribute (LIVE-WIRED this PR, unlike outcome_backend which was scaffolding-only). `DreamRunner.__init__` gains the matching `journal_backend` kwarg. **spec/02 ownership reconciliation:** `spec/02` and `spec/24` both claimed "MemoryBackend retains exclusive ownership of memory/ and journal/" — **both incorrect** as of the legacy codebase; both corrected in this PR to "MemoryBackend retains exclusive ownership of memory/ only; journal/ is carved out to JournalBackend (spec/43)." **spec/35 + wizard.py latent typo fixed:** `journal/*.jsonl` → `journal/*.md` (journal files are always `.md`, never `.jsonl`). Operator override surface: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_JOURNAL_BACKEND` env var (default `"filesystem"`) + `get_default_journal_backend(agent_root)` factory + `doctor.check_journal_backend()` (dual-probe: `list_entries(limit=1)` liveness + `entry.path.read_bytes()` only when entries exist) + public `AtomicAgent.journal_backend` attribute. `JournalExport` is re-exported from the `atomic_agents.export` package root (`from atomic_agents.export import JournalExport`), registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py` + `tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). DRAFT spec/43 ships at 10 normative MUSTs in the Implementer Contract (base-8 PersonaBackend pattern + MUST 9 append atomicity + MUST 10 date-query correctness incl. include-on-unparse fallback). No re-export shim needed: grep confirms no public `atomic_agents.journal` import path existed before this PR (Principle #14 shim mandate is conditional-and-void). Conformance suite: `tests/test_journal_backend_conformance.py` (60 tests, all 10 MUSTs + registry/doctor/golden-render coverage — including byte-identity golden tests for both bundle and agent render formats, plus a `_source_paths`/`_staleness_paths` journal-selection-parity test that pins the cascade `.lease.json` freshness set entry-for-entry against the legacy `rglob` top-N (is_file-filtered) selection; the agent render test drives the REAL `AtomicAgent._load_recent_journal` against a reproduced-legacy reference, not a re-inlined format copy), symlinked-entry SELECTION parity (bundle + dream), the dream dict-adapter shape, a relative-`agent_root` un-resolved-path assertion, a concurrent-append MUST 9 stress test, and a normalized permissions-class `fix_hint` pair asserting `check_journal_backend`'s list-probe and read-probe surface IDENTICAL remediation on a `PermissionError`) + `tests/test_journal_filesystem.py` (filesystem-specific tests: lock placement, month-subdir minting, symlinked-entry SELECTION parity in list/query (legacy followed symlinks), symlinked-`journal/`-DIRECTORY-containment FAIL paths, unresolvable-`agent_root` graceful construction (deferred resolve), symlink-loop list/query `[]` + append fail-loud, `.tmp` sidecar exclusion, skip-unreadable OSError/UnicodeDecodeError, export relative paths, `..` in agent_root rejection). **Resolution-failure guard:** `FilesystemJournalBackend.__init__` defers `agent_root.resolve()` to first use (a lazy `_agent_root_resolved` property) so construction stays side-effect-free and cannot crash agent construction on an unresolvable `agent_root` (symlink loop / inaccessible ancestor); `_journal_dir()` wraps the `.resolve()` calls so `(OSError, RuntimeError)` map to `PathTraversalError`, which reads degrade to `[]` (restoring the legacy `exists()`/`rglob()` graceful-empty behavior) and `append_entry` re-raises (fail-loud). `doctor.check_journal_backend` deliberately does NOT FAIL on an individual symlinked `.md` entry that resolves outside `agent_root` — the runtime follows such an entry through (byte-identity with the legacy `rglob` callers), so doctor agrees with the runtime contract rather than contradict it (`feedback_doctor_dual_probe_pattern`); only a symlinked `journal/` DIRECTORY escape is refused (caught by doctor's direct `_journal_dir()` probe, since `list_entries()` itself returns `[]` on a directory escape). Two MUST 4 fs-specific tests close the append write-failure (`atomic_write` raises `OSError` → propagate, no partial day file) and new-day-into-existing-month-subdir subcases. **Pre-landing review hardening:** the `_safe_read_entry` / `bundle._safe_read_text` graceful-degrade helper (kept byte-identical, [464](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/464)) now (a) guards the `errors="replace"` re-read against a TOCTOU `OSError` so a mid-read deletion degrades-but-keeps instead of crashing the now-hot `agent.call()` journal path, and (b) names the OSError failure mode by exception type only — it no longer embeds `str(exc)` (which carries the absolute filesystem path) into the warning comment that flows into the agent system prompt and out to an external LLM provider. A `dream.start()` real-call-site regression test pins a month-subdir journal entry reaching the pipeline through `query_by_date` as `path.name`. Test suite: 4,180 passed / 72 skipped → **4,252 collected** (+92 net, 2026-06-12).

- **`OutcomeBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemOutcomeBackend` reference impl + DRAFT spec/42** ([426](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/426) — the fifteenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 2). Carves the flat `outcome.py` module into a proper Protocol + storage backend, following the pattern of GoalBackend (spec/41). New `atomic_agents/outcome/` package supersedes `atomic_agents/outcome.py`: `backend.py` (`runtime_checkable OutcomeBackend` Protocol), `types.py` (canonical mutable `OutcomeResult` / `IterationRecord` dataclasses with `to_dict`/`from_dict` + frozen `OutcomeCapabilities` + `OutcomeExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemOutcomeBackend` reference impl), `_outcome_impl.py` (`OutcomeRunner` runtime behavior relocated from flat `outcome.py`), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_outcome_backend` / `get_outcome_backend` / `list_outcome_backends` + `get_default_outcome_backend(agent_root)` factory + backward-compat module-level re-exports). `from atomic_agents.outcome import OutcomeRunner` is a **supported, non-deprecated** compatibility re-export (Principle #14); no `DeprecationWarning`. `OutcomeResult` and `IterationRecord` are **mutable** dataclasses (deliberate divergence from frozen-DTO convention; the outcome layer is a state machine DURING the run — `status`/`explanation`/`iterations`/`output_files` are mutated across the `OutcomeRunner` loop; freezing would break the loop). `OutcomeCapabilities` is frozen (pure value object). `OutcomeExport` carries `(run_id, result_json_bytes, artifact_refs, backend_id, scope)`; `artifact_refs` are relative-to-`agent_root` strings (or absolute fallback when the artifact lives outside the agent vault). `FilesystemOutcomeBackend` implements `Exportable` with `supports_canonical_export=True` and `supports_artifact_storage=True`. **Artifact-reference portability (Option C):** on-disk `result.json` stays **BYTE-IDENTICAL** — `OutcomeRunner._write_result_json` is untouched; the NEW `FilesystemOutcomeBackend.export()` emits portable refs by rebasing absolute artifact paths to relative-to-`agent_root` via `path.is_relative_to(self._agent_root)` guard. `OutcomeCorrupted` exception added to `atomic_agents.exceptions` (mirrors `GoalCorrupted`). Operator override surface: `ATOMIC_AGENTS_OUTCOME_BACKEND` env var + `get_default_outcome_backend(agent_root)` factory + `doctor.check_outcome_backend()` (dual-probe: `list_runs` lightweight + `read_result` only when runs exist) + public `AtomicAgent.outcome_backend` attribute (scaffolding-only this PR — set at construction, never read internally). Do NOT add `outcome_backend=` kwarg to `OutcomeRunner.__init__` until [448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) (the write path is not wired; a kwarg would be dead code). `OutcomeExport` also registered in the spec/40 export conformance harness (`tests/test_export_protocol_conformance.py`) and the capability-advertisement harness (`tests/test_export_capability_advertisement.py`). 9 normative MUSTs in spec/42 Implementer Contract (side-effect-free construction, capability honesty, `list_runs` returns `[]` when absent, atomic writes, round-trip fidelity, `OutcomeCorrupted` on corrupt result.json, `AtomicAgentsError` on absent run, stable `backend_id`, **write-once/result-immutability** as the unique 9th axis — `write_result()` raises on second write for same `run_id`). Conformance suite: `tests/test_outcome_backend_conformance.py` (76 tests, all 9 MUSTs + filesystem-specific + path-traversal/symlink-containment + corruption-net regression tests). Per-iteration telemetry STAYS in `LogBackend` (`PRIMITIVE_OUTCOME_ITERATION` / `_append_iteration_log`) — `OutcomeBackend` covers only the terminal `result.json` envelope. `OutcomeExport` is re-exported from the `atomic_agents.export` package root (`from atomic_agents.export import OutcomeExport`), mirroring `GoalExport`. `export()`/`export_all()` are deliberately scoped to the single most-recent run (the single-run `OutcomeExport` shape) for this scaffolding PR — a documented divergence from the spec/40 `export_all()` "all records" wording; a multi-run export shape is filed as [#454](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/454). Artifact-ref rebasing is against `agent_root` (the agent's own root, for whole-agent portability), a deliberate departure from the arc ruling's literal `agents_root` pattern, recorded in spec/42 §"Artifact-reference portability". DRAFT spec/42 ships at 292 lines; spec/14 §Outcomes cross-reference addendum added; spec/27 `outcome-backend` doctor-check section added (`grep -cE '^def check_[a-z_]+_backend\b'` = 14, `check_outcome_backend` last). The issue body's "spec/13" cross-ref citation is a typo; the correct cross-reference is spec/14 (§Outcomes); the PR body documents the correction. The two pre-existing outcome tests (`tests/test_outcome.py`, `tests/test_goal_outcome_composition.py`) remain UNTOUCHED as the zero-behavior-change regression guard. Goal-outcome coordinator wiring (OutcomeRunner write path through `self.outcome_backend.write_result()`) deferred to [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448). Test suite: 4,088 passed / 72 skipped (net +85 from conformance + export harness + path-traversal/corruption-net regression tests, 2026-06-11).

Changed

- **GCP deployment guides: corrected goals/outcomes protocol status (docs-only).** `extras/gcp/README.md` and `extras/gcp/compute-vm/README.md` still described **goals** and **outcomes** as "no Protocol yet; 383" across their per-layer state-mapping tables (both Cloud Run and Compute-VM topologies), the Group A / Group B split, the Phase 3 scale-out row, and the VM disk-layout tree. Both protocols shipped after those guides were written — `GoalBackend` (DRAFT spec/41, [425](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/425)) and `OutcomeBackend` (DRAFT spec/42, [#426](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/426); write-path wiring deferred to [#448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448)). Updated both surfaces to "Protocol shipped (filesystem reference impl); cloud adapter pending" (mirroring the existing Memory-row phrasing), moved goals/outcomes from Group B (no protocol) to Group A (protocol exists, cloud adapter writable), and split the grouped rows. **As of this PR, `JournalBackend` (DRAFT spec/43, [#427](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/427)) ships, so journal got the same treatment** — its state-table rows in both READMEs move from "no Protocol yet; #383" to "Protocol shipped; cloud adapter pending," journal moves from Group B to Group A, and the Phase 3 row + the VM disk-layout tree line are updated; the "Journal, cascade (383)" adapter-readiness bullet is split so cascade keeps `383` while journal cites `427`/spec/43. Only **dreams** and the **cascade queue** retain the still-accurate "no Protocol yet; 383" status. No code, no import-graph impact. Matches Principle 13 (documentation matches reality, not aspirations).

Fixed

- **`atomic-agents init` templates now pass the real `doctor` write-paths check — framework-wide relative tools.md path anchoring + write-path directory creation** ([541](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/541)). Two bugs made a freshly-scaffolded agent fail `doctor` immediately. **(1) CWD-anchoring:** `parse_tools_md`/`parse_tools_md_text` resolved bare-relative path tokens (`memory/`, `journal/`) against the process CWD instead of the agent folder, so `doctor` (run from anywhere but the agent dir) reported every write_path "does not exist." Fixed with `resolve_under_agent_root()` (`_platform.py`): a bare-relative token resolves under `agent_root`; absolute (`/…`) and tilde (`~/…`) tokens pass through `expand()` unchanged (backward-compatible). The parser threads `agent_root` and emits a DEBUG audit log line per anchored token (DEBUG, not WARNING: bare-relative is the canonical default-template shape, so anchoring is the correct happy path — a WARNING would spam stderr on every agent load / doctor run); callers without `agent_root` in scope (the DB-only `AgentProfile.from_dict` round-trip) keep CWD-anchoring with a `UserWarning`, per the documented contract. `agent_root` is threaded at every live call site (`agent.py` runtime config via `profile/filesystem.py:load_profile`, `doctor._safe_parse_tools`, `tuning._load_write_paths`). **(2) Missing write-path dirs:** the scaffold hardcoded a fixed subdir set (`journal/`, `log/`, `output/`) and never created the `writer` template's declared `drafts/` + `revisions/`, so `writer` failed `doctor` with "drafts (does not exist); revisions (does not exist)." Fixed by deriving the dir set from the just-rendered `tools.md` write_paths (`_create_empty_dirs` parses it and `mkdir`s each), so ANY template's declared write-paths exist and a future template author adding a write-path bullet "just works." **Security (path traversal):** every parsed write_path is re-validated with `_io.safe_resolve_under` against `agent_root` before `mkdir` — a bullet resolving OUTSIDE the agent folder (absolute, `~`, or `../escape`) is REFUSED (never created) with a warning; the wizard only ever provisions directories inside the agent it scaffolds. The same `_create_empty_dirs` runs after a successful Add-to-it merge so a backfilled `tools.md` keeps its write-path dirs in lockstep. **Conformance (the structural fix):** `tests/test_init_doctor_conformance.py` scaffolds each template (`advisor`/`researcher`/`writer`) AND the interactive Q&A path onto a real tmpdir, runs the REAL (un-mocked) `doctor` from a decoy CWD that is NOT the agent folder, and asserts `overall_exit_code == 0` with no write-paths FAIL — closing the gap where every prior init smoke test mocked `doctor → PASS`, which is why this class of bug escaped to production. The interactive conformance test surfaced and fixed a pre-existing latent crash: the Q4 autonomy table passed `max_width=` to `rich.table.Table`, which has no such kwarg (`width=` is correct) — every real interactive init in a non-dumb terminal would have crashed at Q4. New `_create_empty_dirs` unit tests cover the parse-driven mkdir, a missing-bullet negative control, and the path-traversal refusal (with per-invocation negative controls verifying each guard bites). The framework-wide anchoring decision (the `resolve_under_agent_root` + backward-compat detection guard) is independent of [#544](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/544); documenting the bare-relative-anchors-to-`agent_root` convention in the LOCKED `spec/01` (which today only shows the placeholder-absolute `<agents_root>/<name>/…` form) is tracked as a spec-LOCK-ceremony follow-up in [#549](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/549). Closes #541.

- **GCP Secret Manager docs corrected post-340 (stale "backend does not exist" drift removed)** ([344](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/344) readiness scorecard). `extras/gcp/secret-manager-bootstrap.sh`, `extras/gcp/README.md`, and `extras/gcp/cloudrun-service.yaml` still told operators "GCPSecretManagerBackend does NOT yet exist" and "Do NOT set `ATOMIC_AGENTS_SECRET_BACKEND=gcp`" — both false since the GCP Secret Manager backend shipped at #340 PR 2 (spec/38 LOCKED). The bootstrap header + body now document the two supported delivery paths (PATH 1: `--set-secrets` env-var injection via the default `FilesystemSecretBackend`; PATH 2: live resolution via `ATOMIC_AGENTS_SECRET_BACKEND=gcp` + `ATOMIC_AGENTS_SECRET_BACKEND_URL=projects/<project-id>/secrets`, requiring the `[gcp]` extra), the README deploy diagram reflects the shipped backend, and the `cloudrun-service.yaml` secrets comment matches. All env-var names + the URL format were verified against `atomic_agents/secret_backend/` (cross-family Codex review caught the `cloudrun-service.yaml` residual a first-pass grep missed). Surfaced by the 344 GCP delivery readiness scorecard, which also filed [526](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/526) (Redis/Postgres `IdempotencyBackend` for cross-restart dedup on Cloud Run).

- **`GoalManager.has_goal()` tightened to `is_file()` for fail-closed boundary / MUST-9 alignment on degenerate `goal.md` shapes** ([494](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/494)). `has_goal()` previously used `Path.exists()`, which returns `True` for a directory at `goal.md`. All four call sites (`has_active_goal`, `load`, `archive` precheck, CLI `next`) are strictly safer under `is_file()`: a directory-shaped `goal.md` is now treated as absent everywhere, matching `FilesystemGoalBackend` MUST-9's `is_file()` semantics. Regression: `test_has_goal_returns_false_for_directory_shaped_goal_md` — a directory at `goal.md` → `has_goal() is False` AND `archive()` raises `AtomicAgentsError("No active goal to archive")` at the GoalManager precheck (the degenerate shape no prior test exercised). Docstring at `test_goal_adoption_golden.py::test_has_goal_works_after_backend_injection` updated to reflect the `is_file()` probe. No spec change (GoalManager is not a Protocol surface; the fix aligns it with EXISTING spec/41 MUST-9). Test suite: **4,506 collected** (golden 35→36, 2026-06-14).

- **`doctor` no-`--agent` mode now emits SKIP for `goal-backend`, `outcome-backend`, and `journal-backend`.** The no-agent enumeration in `run_doctor()` was missing the agent-scoped backend checks, so `run_doctor(agent_name=None)` silently produced *no* entry for them (the other backends correctly emit SKIP). The guard test `test_run_doctor_no_agent_skips_agent_checks` used a subset assertion that did not assert them, so the omission passed blind (the exact silent-omission false-PASS class the test's own docstring claims to defend against). `goal-backend` was a pre-existing miss from 425; `outcome-backend` was introduced by 426; `journal-backend` would have been introduced by this PR. All three are now in the SKIP enumeration and the test's assertion set. `check_journal_backend` also normalizes its `list_entries` and `read_bytes` probes to a single shared permissions-class `fix_hint` so a `PermissionError` surfaces identical remediation regardless of which probe trips first.

Security

- **Dashboard escapes log-derived model IDs (stored-XSS hardening)** ([517](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/517)). `dashboard/render.py` interpolated run-log `model` strings into HTML at four boundaries without `html.escape()`: the `_model_pill` span label (covering the global recent-runs table, the per-agent model-mix table, and the per-agent top-runs table), the global per-agent model-pill inline span, the model-mix bar segment label, and the model-mix bar `title="{model}"` attribute (where a raw `"` breaks out of the attribute). A model id written into the JSONL log as `zz"><img src=x onerror=...>` would render as live markup in a multi-viewer / served dashboard. Under the single-user filesystem shape this sits inside the T15 vault-writer-trusted boundary; it matters in the fleet/served-dashboard shape, and the existing CSP (#400) mitigates but should not be the only defense. Fixed by escaping at every HTML construction boundary (the display helper `_short_model_name` stays plain-text so the pill path never double-escapes). Two regression tests (`test_xss_model_id_escaped_in_global_dashboard`, `test_xss_model_id_escaped_in_per_agent_dashboard`); each of the four escape sites was confirmed load-bearing by an individual negative-control strip (strip → a test reddens; restore → green). Cross-family (Codex) + fresh-Opus adversarial review both returned no findings on completeness or double-escape.

- **`FilesystemGoalBackend` symlink-containment guard — brought to parity with its sibling backends** ([448](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/448) PR1). #448 PR1 makes `load_goal`/`save_goal`/`append_history_event` load-bearing for the first time (they were decorative scaffolding from 425), so `/ship`'s security review flagged that `FilesystemGoalBackend` was the one backend in the 425–428 wave that shipped WITHOUT the perimeter-containment guard its siblings have (`FilesystemOutcomeBackend._runs_root()`, `FilesystemJournalBackend._journal_dir()`). A symlinked `goal.md` / `goal_history.jsonl` / `goal_archive/` / `.goal.lock` pointing OUTSIDE the resolved `agent_root` would otherwise be followed on read/write — a perimeter escape (a write landing outside the agent vault). Fixed with ONE consolidated invariant `_require_within_root(path, label)` (resolve + `is_relative_to(agent_root)` → `PathTraversalError`, folding resolution failure — symlink loop / inaccessible ancestor — into `PathTraversalError` rather than crashing agent construction now that `get_default_goal_backend()` runs in `AtomicAgent.__init__`), applied at EVERY I/O boundary: `load_goal`, `_write_goal` (save), `_append_jsonl` (history), `_goal_lock`, `archive_goal`, `list_archived`, `read_schema_version`, `goal_text`, `export`. Consolidated (one invariant, all paths) per `feedback_containment_reframe_not_whackamole`, NOT per-path point-checks. Zero behavior change for legitimate paths (the guard only fires on attack-shaped symlinks; the macOS `/var`→`/private/var` symlink is handled because both sides are resolved). New regression suite `tests/test_goal_backend_containment.py` (12 tests: per-path symlinked-escape → `PathTraversalError`, plus a positive control proving a normal vault never trips the guard). Cross-family Grok review confirmed the guard is sound for its perimeter contract; the residual within-perimeter TOCTOU (symlink swap between resolve and write) is the framework-wide [480](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/480) item, explicitly out of scope per the trust model (an actor who can plant a symlink inside `agent_root` is already inside the trust boundary). The live in-place `GoalManager.archive()` path (unresolved `agent_root`, pre-existing) is the distinct companion gap, filed as [#486](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/486).

- **`FilesystemQueueBackend` perimeter containment hardened to a single sound invariant** ([428](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/428)). The pre-carve `_cascade.py` queue had **no** path containment at all; carving it into a Protocol newly exposed it as a backend surface, so `/ship`'s multi-round adversarial review (Claude subagent + cross-family Codex) reproduced and closed every escape *outside* `project_root` — the sole in-scope security boundary. Closed and regression-tested: per-child symlinked `claimed/<token>/` dir leaking/exfiltrating via `list_claimed`/`recover` (the #426 ancestor-escalation class); `original_name` path-traversal in `release`/`move_to_dead_letter`/`renew_lease`; a symlinked source file claimed by `claim_next`; the `_cascade.py` shims bypassing validation by calling the `_at_path` sinks directly; a forged `QueueItem` whose `path` or source-*state* (`claimed/` vs `done/`/`dead-letter/`) escaped; symlinked work-file *leaves* and symlinked parent state dirs; and a symlinked `.lease.json` sidecar read outside the project. The accumulated point-checks were then consolidated into **one** invariant — `_require_canonical_source()`: a work file's RESOLVED path must equal EXACTLY its canonical location under the resolved queue root, be a regular file, and not traverse a symlink (exact-canonical-equality subsumes containment + source-state + basename + symlink-leaf + symlinked-parent). spec/44 gained a **"Security model — trust boundary"** section: the boundary is `project_root`; within-queue state integrity against an actor who already has write access to `queue/` is explicitly **out of scope** (that actor is inside the trust zone and can corrupt state directly), and adversarial/multi-host deployments use a real-authz backend (Postgres/Redis/SQS per T4), not a hardened filesystem backend. Residual within-perimeter items tracked as follow-ups: [473](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/473) (renew_lease shim, frozen signature), [#477](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/477) (export `rglob` symlinked-subdir DoS), [#478](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/478) (claim-token clobber), [#479](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/479) (sidecar write symlink-leaf), [#480](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/480) (framework-wide `openat`/`O_NOFOLLOW` dir-fd TOCTOU hardening). No live vulnerability — the queue has no internal runtime caller this PR; this hardens the newly-exposed surface before any consumer wires it.

- **`FilesystemOutcomeBackend` refuses path traversal and symlink escape on `run_id` → path handling**, matching the framework invariant "Path traversal refused per `_io.safe_resolve_under`" and the sibling `memory/filesystem.py`. `write_result`/`read_result` validate `run_id` via `safe_resolve_under` + a single-direct-child check (refusing `../escape`, absolute paths, and in-root degenerate `''`/`'.'`/nested `a/b`); `list_runs`/`export`/`read_result` refuse symlinked run directories, symlinked `result.json`, and a symlinked `outcomes`/`runs` **ancestor** (containment anchored at `agent_root.resolve()` so a symlinked ancestor cannot become the trusted root). `read_result` and `export` also reject valid-JSON-wrong-shape `result.json` (a list/scalar, or non-list `output_files`/`iterations`) as `OutcomeCorrupted` / degrade-to-empty instead of leaking `AttributeError`. Hardening of a new, not-yet-runtime-wired surface (no live vulnerability); the doctor check fail-closes a traversing run rather than masking it as a benign vanished-run race.

- **`GoalBackend` Protocol + `FilesystemGoalBackend` reference impl + DRAFT spec/41** ([425](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/425) — the fourteenth backend Protocol, PR 1 of 1). Carves the flat `goal.py` module (GoalManager, Goal/SubGoal dataclasses, CLI, schema constants all in one file) into a proper Protocol + storage backend, following the pattern of MemoryBackend (spec/20), LogBackend (spec/22), and SecretBackend (spec/38). New `atomic_agents/goal/` package: `backend.py` (`runtime_checkable GoalBackend` Protocol), `types.py` (canonical mutable `Goal`/`SubGoal` dataclasses + single `validate_goal()` + `GoalCapabilities` + `GoalExport` as an `ExportableResult` subclass), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemGoalBackend` reference impl), `__init__.py` (registry: `register_goal_backend` / `get_goal_backend` / `list_goal_backends` + `get_default_goal_backend(scope)` factory). `GoalManager` relocated to `_goal_impl.py`; the documented public `from atomic_agents.goal import GoalManager` import path is preserved as a **supported, non-deprecated** compatibility re-export (no `DeprecationWarning` — path is intentionally permanent, matching Principle #14). `apply_transition(sub_goal_id, to_status, fields)` is the load-bearing atomic Protocol method: one durable status flip + optional output/blocked_by fields + one `goal_history.jsonl` audit line under a filesystem file lock (the same atomicity guarantee a Postgres backend delivers via a SQL transaction — this is what puts it on the Protocol per the T15 ruling).
