- **PolicyBackend cost-cap consumption + PolicySnapshot per-call machinery + MandateCheck integration + `policy_decision` event emission** ([89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 3a of 4**). Cost caps are now LIVE — operators authoring `<project_root>/policy.md` with `cost_caps.daily_usd: 50` see fleet caps enforce on real money from this PR forward (closes the operator-multi-agent pain that was most acute today: fleet operators hand-syncing `model.md` budgets across N agents). PR 3b ships the non-cap surfaces (tool/MCP/model) in log-only mode behind `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` flag — separate PR. **Three bisectable commits:**

- **(1) Schema hygiene + validator + spec/32 amendments.** Per Dan's locked NEW DECISIONS resolved during plan-subagent prep: (D1) DEFER `cumulative_usd` cap dimension to v1.1 — dropped from `CostCaps`, `PolicyDecision.cap_dimension` Literal, `PolicySnapshot`, `AgentPolicyOverride`, parser, conformance tests, and spec/32 prose. Policy v1 ships with daily + monthly only, matching what `model.md cost_guardrails` already supports. (D2) LOOSEN `_validate_agent_name` to permit `[a-zA-Z0-9._+-]+` (was `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+` strictly) — preserves existing operator deployments using dots / at-signs / plus / etc. in agent names; still rejects path-traversal tokens, control chars, newlines, leading dots, empty. (F6 plan-subagent finding) ADD `enforced: bool = False` field to `PolicyDecision` — semantics: `enforced=True` means denial blocked the action / override took effect; `enforced=False` means log-only (denial recorded but action proceeded). Cost-cap denials always emit `enforced=True`. PR 3b ships non-cap surfaces in log-only mode. Spec/32 §"Policy decision event schema" updated; new §"`enforced` field semantics" + §"`agent_name` character set" + §"Denying layer resolution" sections.

- **(2) Cascade-aware policy.md scoping (closes [236](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/236)).** PR 2's wiring used `self.agents_root` as the default scope_root. In cascaded layouts (`<system>/projects/<project>/agents/<role>/`), `self.agents_root` resolves to `<system>/projects/<project>/agents/` — NOT `cascade.project_root` (`<system>/projects/<project>/`). For cascade operators relying on defaults, PR 3 consumption would have silently missed the operator's policy.md, breaking the cost-cap-always-enforce promise for cascade deployments. The fix: `_init_policy_backend` now uses `cascade.project_root if self.cascade is not None else self.agents_root` as the default scope_root. Operator-supplied `policy_backend=...` kwarg STILL wins (programmatic-override discipline). `doctor.check_policy_backend` adds a cascade-aware warning per F12: when agent is in cascade layout AND `agent.policy_backend._project_root != cascade.project_root` (filesystem backend only; skip for SaaS/Postgres), emit WARN with remediation message. New tests pin: cascade layout uses cascade.project_root; non-cascade preserves PR 2 behavior; doctor warns on cascade scope mismatch when operator supplies explicit FilesystemPolicyBackend(agents_root).

- **(3) Cost-cap consumption — the body.** New `PolicySnapshotForCall` frozen dataclass in `atomic_agents/policy/types.py` — per-call snapshot taken at `agent.call()` entry per design Premise 3 ("snapshot at call entry; consulted from snapshot for rest of call"). The snapshot is FROZEN for the duration of the call — operator edits to `policy.md` mid-call are deferred to the NEXT call (predictability over freshness within the call). `_take_policy_snapshot()` helper on AtomicAgent constructs the snapshot from the backend; called at `call()` entry alongside the other `_*_this_run` resets; cleared in finally block. **`_check_cost_guardrails` integration:** Policy daily + monthly caps MIN-compose with `model.md cost_guardrails` per-dimension (D2 — daily and monthly compose independently). When the cap fires due to Policy contributing, emit a `PolicyDecision` event with `denying_layer="policy"`, `axis="cost_cap"`, `cap_dimension="daily|monthly"`, populated `attempted_value` + `effective_cap`, `enforced=True`, `cache_ttl_s` from the snapshot. Tiebreaker per F14: when Policy + model.md tie, `_resolve_denying_layer` picks "policy" (most-fleet first; tiebreaker order policy > model_md). New helpers in `agent.py`: `_min_or_other(a, b)` (None-aware MIN — None means "no opinion at this layer"), `_resolve_denying_layer(...)` (the tiebreak resolver). **MandateCheck integration (F7):** `MandateCheck.__init__` accepts new `policy_effective_caps: CostCaps | None = None` kwarg. Steps 7 (token budget) + 8 (external budget) MIN-compose mandate's per-cap-kind values with Policy's daily + monthly. `agent.py:_ensure_mandate_check` passes `policy_effective_caps=self._policy_snapshot_this_call.effective_caps` so MandateCheck steps 7-8 share the same arithmetic as `_check_cost_guardrails`. **`policy_decision` event emission infrastructure:** `PRIMITIVE_POLICY_DECISION = "policy_decision"` added to `logs/types.py`; `_emit_policy_decision(decision, log_backend, run_id)` helper in `policy/types.py` builds a `RunRecord` from the `PolicyDecision` and calls `log_backend.append(record)`. Wire shape per spec/32 §"PolicyDecision LogBackend envelope": `primitive="policy_decision"`, `trigger="policy_decision"`, `extra=<all PolicyDecision fields>`. Operators query: `LogQuery(primitive="policy_decision")` or filter by `extra["decision_kind"]` for deny vs override. **New `tests/test_policy_cost_cap_consumption.py`** (12 tests): snapshot lifecycle (none-before-call, take-returns-correct-shape, no-policy.md is no-opinion), MIN composition helpers (`_min_or_other`, `_resolve_denying_layer` tiebreak), MandateCheck kwarg surface (accepts + defaults), `policy_decision` emission shape (deny + override kinds, RunRecord envelope), end-to-end snapshot drives effective cap, no-policy.md zero-behavior-change pin.

**Plan-subagent pre-impl review:** 18 findings (5 SEVERE + 7 HIGH + 4 MED + 2 LOW). Surfaced 3 NEW architectural decisions for Dan (cumulative cap dimension v1 OR defer; agent_name validator strict OR loosened; PR 3 single OR split into 3a+3b) — all 3 resolved before impl. F1 cascade scoping was the highest-stakes finding (would have silently broken cost-cap-always-enforce for cascade operators); folded into commit 2. F2 PolicySnapshot per-call machinery was THE load-bearing structural finding (Premise 3 was unimplementable as-written without a per-call attribute); resolved via the `_policy_snapshot_this_call` attribute pattern matching `_helpers_this_run`/`_delegations_this_run`/`_mandate_allowed_proposals` precedents. F7 MandateCheck integration resolved via kwarg threading (clean — re-instantiate not required because MandateCheck constructor is cheap + the kwarg-store + step-7/8 read pattern stays linear).

**Methodology that compounded:** plan-subagent dispatch (Sonnet, ~7-min wall-clock) caught risks worth 3+ re-architecture cycles. Two prior PR-3a implementation subagents hit session limits mid-impl (each completing partial work — schema + wiring — before timing out); third subagent picked up the integration; final inline pass wrote the dedicated tests + CHANGELOG + commit. The methodology track record holds: subagent dispatch works best for clearly-bounded scopes; when an impl spans 1500+ LOC across 5 files, the safer pattern is splitting (D3's PR 3a + 3b split was driven by this exact observation).

**Zero behavior change** for operators WITHOUT `policy.md` — snapshot has all-None `CostCaps()`, `_min_or_other(model_md_value, None)` returns `model_md_value` unchanged, existing cap-check arithmetic preserved. The 115 existing `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites + every test in the suite continues to produce identical pre-PR-3a results. Test count: 2323 → **2344 passing** (+21 net: ~9 from commits 1-2 + 12 from commit 3) + 34 skipped.

- **`PolicyBackend` wired through `AtomicAgent.__init__` + per-runner kwargs + delegate threading + `doctor.check_policy_backend`** ([89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 2 of 4**). The Protocol surface shipped in PR 1 is now consumed by the framework's bootstrap path. `AtomicAgent.__init__` accepts a new keyword-only `policy_backend: PolicyBackend | None = None` kwarg; default resolves via `get_default_policy_backend(self.agents_root)` honoring the `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_BACKEND` env var. **`self.agents_root`** is the scope (NOT `self.agent_root` like Mandate) because Policy is fleet-shaped: a single `policy.md` at the project root applies to all agents per spec/32 §"Locked Answer — policy.md Placement" (the wrong choice would have silently no-opted every Policy lookup in PR 3 consumption). Public `self.policy_backend` attribute mirrors `self.log_backend` / `self.profile_backend` / `self.tool_registry_backend` / `self.mandate_backend`. Per-runner kwargs added to `OutcomeRunner` + `EvalRunner` + `DreamRunner` (DreamRunner stores for API parity but doesn't construct internal AtomicAgents — same shape as the other agent-internal backends). **`delegate.py` threads `policy_backend=self.policy_backend` per /plan-eng-review D1** — the coordinator's PolicyBackend instance flows into delegate-target AtomicAgent construction by reference. This is **the second fleet-scoped backend that threads to delegates** (Profile was the first; Lock + Log + ToolRegistry + Mandate are per-agent and deliberately don't thread). Without threading, an operator's custom PolicyBackend (Postgres SaaS, org-admin-console) would be bypassed in delegated work, defeating the entire purpose of fleet-wide Policy. `doctor.check_policy_backend` coherence check landed in `doctor.py` with PASS/WARN/FAIL ladder + capability snapshot (reads `cache_ttl_s` + `durable` from `backend.capabilities()` directly per plan-subagent F7 — never hard-coded; honest if a SaaS backend reports cache_ttl_s=60) + URL credential redaction inline (mirrors the 4-prior-check pattern; `password OR username` check covers token-as-username managed-service URLs) + `policy_md_exists: bool` informational signal in detail dict. PASS when backend resolves + policy.md exists; WARN when backend resolves but policy.md is absent (operator hasn't authored Policy yet — informational); FAIL on `BackendNotRegistered` (typo'd env var) or capability-probe failure. Wired into `run_doctor` as a per-agent check + added to the no-agent SKIP enumeration. `render_human`'s badge dict gained the missing `WARN: "[warn]"` entry (pre-existing latent bug — other checks emit WARN but the test surface hadn't triggered the render path until `check_policy_backend` did). New `tests/test_policy_integration.py` (14 integration tests) covers default-resolution, explicit-kwarg-wins, env-var dispatch, public-attribute discipline, explicit-None triggers default, zero-behavior-change for existing construction sites, all 3 runners' kwarg threading via the established monkeypatch-AtomicAgent-class pattern (captures kwargs at the boundary — canonical defense against the kwarg-drop trap), DreamRunner API-parity storage-only, **delegate identity-threading test** (`target.policy_backend is coordinator.policy_backend` — SAME INSTANCE, not just same type; the load-bearing D1 assertion per plan-subagent F3 — a future contributor refactoring `delegate()` who drops the threading would still pass an `isinstance` check, but the `is` identity test catches the regression loudly), two-agents-share-no-state isolation pin, doctor PASS/FAIL/WARN paths. Class-level type annotation `policy_backend: PolicyBackend` added to `AtomicAgent` for static-analysis narrowing — without this, mypy/pyright would narrow `agent.policy_backend` to the concrete `FilesystemPolicyBackend` default rather than the `PolicyBackend` Protocol, breaking SaaS/Postgres/org-admin-console downstream type-checking. **Zero behavior change** for existing operator deployments — `self.policy_backend` exists on every constructed AtomicAgent but NO call site reads it (PR 3 wires consumption via `_check_cost_guardrails` MIN composition + tool dispatch site + MCP discovery site + model selection site + `policy_decision` event emission behind `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_ENFORCE_NONCAP=false` flag). All 115 `AtomicAgent(...)` construction sites in the test suite see byte-identical pre-PR-2 behavior. Test count: 2309 → **2323 passing** (+14 net) + 34 skipped. Parallelization: 4 Sonnet sub-agents on independent files (agent.py + runners + doctor + tests) completed in ~3 min wall-clock vs ~45 min sequential; integration phase caught 2 issues (doctor's `badge` dict missing `WARN` key — pre-existing latent; Lane B's TYPE_CHECKING block placement in `eval.py` was structurally identical to the pre-existing pattern in `outcome.py`/`dream.py` so no E402 regression). Plan-subagent pre-impl review caught 3 SEVERE + 7 HIGH + 3 MED + 1 LOW with ZERO new architectural decisions — all 14 folded into the lane briefs before dispatch. Key shape-corrections: use `self.agents_root` not `self.agent_root` (F1); thread in `agent.py:delegate()` method not `delegate.py` module (F10); identity-pinning test not isinstance (F3); doctor SKIP enumeration + dispatch (F6 — also surfaced that `check_mandate_backend` is missing from doctor.py as a regression from #124 arc; follow-up issue to be filed inline per scope discipline).

- **`PolicyBackend` Protocol scaffolding + `FilesystemPolicyBackend` reference impl + `policy.md` parser + parametrized conformance suite + spec/32 RFC** ([89](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/89) — PolicyBackend protocol arc **PR 1 of 4**). Scaffolding-only PR: no call site routes through the new Protocol yet (`AtomicAgent.__init__` is unchanged); PR 2 wires the bootstrap path; PR 3 wires consumption (cost-cap MIN integration + tool/MCP/model log-only mode + `policy_decision` event emission); PR 4 spec lock + flag-flip ungate + status flip to "nine backend protocols shipped". The new module establishes the operator-facing surface for the cross-agent configuration layer that PRs 2-4 build on. New package `atomic_agents/policy/` follows the 8-backend established pattern (Memory / LLM / Judge / Lock / Log / AgentProfile / ToolRegistry / Mandate): `types.py` (canonical frozen dataclasses `CostCaps`, `PolicyCapabilities` with `cache_ttl_s: int | None` per /plan-eng-review D5, `PolicyDecision` log-event dataclass as discriminated union with `decision_kind: Literal["deny", "override"]` discriminator + axis-specific fields per /plan-eng-review D9 fold #4 + plan-subagent F5 — SaaS / Postgres adapters target the schema from day 1, exception hierarchy `PolicyError` / `PolicyInvalid`), `backend.py` (`runtime_checkable` `PolicyBackend` Protocol with 4 query methods + `capabilities()` + registry primitives `register_policy_backend` / `unregister_policy_backend` / `get_policy_backend` / `list_policy_backends` / `get_default_policy_backend` honoring `ATOMIC_AGENTS_POLICY_BACKEND` env var — `unregister_*` matches the standard registry surface from Lock/Log/Profile/LLM/Judge), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemPolicyBackend(project_root)` reference impl with path-traversal refusal at the API boundary per spec/32 MUST 1, per-agent isolation enforced at the storage layer per MUST 2, **construction is side-effect-free** per MUST 4 — no stat, no parse, no env-var validation at `__init__`; first method call lazy-parses, **mtime+size-gated parse cache** keyed on `(st_mtime_ns, st_size)` composite per plan-subagent F8 — catches same-second edits on 1-second-mtime-granularity filesystems (HFS+, ext4 without sub-second timestamps) via size proxy; concurrent parse is idempotent — no lock per plan-subagent F10; capability advertises `cache_ttl_s=0` per plan-subagent F1 reconciliation — "operator-observable upper bound on staleness at the API boundary" semantic; URL factory `make_filesystem_policy_backend_from_url("filesystem:///path")` with credential-redacted `ValueError` per MUST 6). New top-level `atomic_agents/policy_md.py` parser supporting BOTH top-level fleet-default fields (cost_caps, tools, mcp_servers, model) AND a nested `agents: { <agent_name>: { ... } }` section for per-agent overrides per /plan-eng-review D7 — Premise 5 revised; mirrors `mandates_md.py` parser shape but without state machinery (Policy has no revocation, no expiry, no reservations). **Parser composition** per plan-subagent F7 resolution: cost caps MIN per dimension; tool / MCP allowlist field-merge with deny-takes-precedence within layer (effective_allow = fleet.allow ∪ agent.allow; effective_deny = fleet.deny ∪ agent.deny; is_allowed = NOT in effective_deny AND (effective_allow empty OR in effective_allow)); model selection per-agent REPLACES fleet; empty `agents: foo: {}` body means no override per plan-subagent F12 — fleet defaults apply (explicit revocation requires explicit `null` per field). Parametrized conformance suite `tests/test_policy_protocol_conformance.py` (~30 conformance tests across `FilesystemPolicyBackend` + `MockPolicyBackend` registered under `mock:` backend_id via pytest fixture with **register-in-setup + unregister-in-teardown** discipline per plan-subagent F3 — prevents cross-suite registry pollution) — each test pins a spec/32 invariant (construction-side-effect-free, no-policy-md returns no-opinion, `agent_name` path-traversal/control/newline/empty/leading-dot refusal across all 4 query methods, tool/server name validation accepts dots+dashes+colons, fleet-cost-caps apply to unmentioned agent, per-agent cap MIN composes with fleet, fleet tools.allow restricts unknown tools, fleet tools.deny overrides allow, per-agent tool allow merges with fleet, per-agent tool deny unions with fleet, model override replaces fleet, empty `agents:` body means no override). `tests/test_policy_filesystem_backend.py` (~15 filesystem-specific tests covering constructor accepts non-existent path, constructor does not stat filesystem, first method call lazy-parses, mtime-cache hit skips parse, mtime-cache invalidates on mtime change, size-change invalidates even when mtime same — the F8 same-second-edit defense, concurrent parse after mtime bump is idempotent — F10 5-thread regression test, malformed policy.md raises `PolicyInvalid` on first call NOT construction, URL factory accepts non-existent path, URL factory refuses other-scheme URLs with credential redaction — F6 / MUST 6 defense). **New `docs/spec/32-policy-backend.md`** lands as RFC (locks at PR 4 when implementation matches and conformance suite pins the contract). §"Implementer contract for policy backends" enumerates **7 normative MUSTs** derived from policy semantics (Policy has no state, no reservations, no lifecycle events beyond `policy_decision`, so fewer MUSTs than spec/22/24/25/29's 8): (1) `agent_name` validation at API boundary, (2) per-agent isolation, (3) fresh re-read with `cache_ttl_s`-bounded staleness, (4) construction side-effect-free, (5) capability honesty, (6) URL credential redaction, (7) `PolicyDecision` event schema compliance. - **`atomic-agents bundle` CLI command + spec/26 cascade-bundle pre-render for skill-mode loads** ([231](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/231)). Filed from Muse Showrunner smoke test 2026-05-17 — end-of-session run took ~7 minutes wall time, dominated by per-file Read tool round-trips during skill-mode cascade load (30-90s before first substantive response on every invocation). The fix: a small framework primitive that pre-renders the cascade into one file the skill loads via a single Read. Wall-time for cascade load drops from 30-90s to 1-3s on a realistic 18-24 file cascade. **New module** `atomic_agents/bundle.py` (~430 LOC) composes the existing `_cascade.py` detect_cascade + resolve_tools_md + load_role_prompt + load_project_layer functions plus filesystem walks for memory INDEX + pinned + recent atomic notes + wiki INDEX + recent journal — exactly the spec/04 steps [1]-[10] (single-agent) or spec/06 three-layer equivalent (cascaded) that `AtomicAgent.load()` would assemble at startup. Section headers mirror spec/04 §"Cache breakpoints" so a future caller can map bundle sections back to Anthropic prompt-cache breakpoints by parsing the headers. Source files stay canonical (rule #1 — vault is source of truth); bundles are derived artifacts cached at `$ATOMIC_AGENTS_CACHE_DIR/<slug>.md` (default `~/.cache/atomic-agents/bundles/`). Atomic writes via `_io.atomic_write` — bundle path is either fully rewritten or unchanged. **New CLI subcommand** `atomic-agents bundle <agent>` in `atomic_agents/cli.py` with `--if-stale` (default skill-mode invocation — regenerates only when any source file's mtime exceeds the bundle's; sub-millisecond check on local SSD), `--refresh` (force regeneration), `--extra-file <path>` repeatable for operator-supplied extras, `--to-stdout` / `--print-path` / `--cache-dir` / `--agents-root`. **Operator extras mechanism** for skill-specific augments (the motivating case: Muse Showrunner reads `~/ObsidianVault/personal/identity/{SOUL,USER,HEARTBEAT}.md` which are NOT part of the framework cascade): declarative `<agent>/bundle.md` listing one path per line (supports `- path` markdown-list shape, ` comment` lines, backtick-quoted paths, globs) OR repeatable `--extra-file` CLI flag. Missing extras raise `FileNotFoundError` — no silent drops; operators learn about misspellings immediately. **No file watcher** — the prior session's draft referenced "existing file-watching infrastructure" which doesn't exist in atomic-agents-stack (verified by searching `watchdog` / `inotify` / `fswatch`); mtime comparison runs inside `--if-stale` and is invisible in the skill's wall-time. **No new backend protocol** — bundles are a *derived rendering* of state behind existing protocols (`MemoryBackend`, `AgentProfileBackend`); per CLAUDE.md rule 2, protocols exist for storage primitives, and pre-rendering is not a storage primitive. Future `PersonaBackend` (62) composes cleanly: when it ships, the persona section of the bundle routes through it via an implementation-internal edit to `bundle.py`'s render functions; the bundle's external interface (CLI flags, file format, cache layout) stays stable. **New `docs/spec/26-cascade-bundle.md`** (DRAFT, locks when the implementation matches and tests pass) documents the 6 load-bearing decisions, the bundle file format with header + section markers, the contract `render_bundle` honors, the staleness model, the extras mechanism (including the trust model — operators reading their own filesystem; no privilege boundary), the composition path for future backends, and explicit non-goals (no watcher, no on-demand-content bundling, no new backend protocol, no bundle-aware runtime, no multi-agent bundles). **New doctor check** `check_bundle_cache_writable` (host-level, no `--agent` required since cache is per-operator-machine) validates the bundle cache directory exists and is writable with `fix_hint` for the two failure modes (unreadable dir → `chmod`; `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CACHE_DIR` points somewhere unwritable). **New tests** `tests/test_cascade_bundle.py` (35 tests, split across two commits): the initial 18 cover cascaded full layout, flat single-agent layout, tools.override merging, missing-files graceful omission, bundle.md declarative extras (with comments + list shape + globs), --extra-file CLI extras, missing-extras-raises, --if-stale skip when fresh, --if-stale regen on source mutation, atomic write (monkeypatched mid-write failure leaves no partial file), pinned-note detection via `pinned: true` frontmatter, recent-notes selection excluding pinned, recent-journal selection newest-first, `ATOMIC_AGENTS_CACHE_DIR` env var override, slug generation under agents-root, slug fallback when agent path is not under agents-root. A follow-up commit adds 17 more closing the coverage gaps flagged by /ship's subagent coverage audit (raised from 62% to ~90%): CLI subcommand coverage (`--print-path` no-regen, `--to-stdout`, missing-agent → exit 1, missing-extra-file → exit 1, `--if-stale`/`--refresh` argparse mutex), `check_bundle_cache_writable` PASS / mkdir-OSError / probe-write-OSError / env-var-source-string, and bundle.md edge cases (self-mutation triggering staleness, glob-matches-zero raising, relative-path resolution under agent_root, backtick-quoted paths, `pinned: yes` and `pinned: 1` truthy alternatives accepted alongside `pinned: true`). **Skill template doc updated** at `docs/implementation/claude-skill-agent.md` to show the bundle-then-Read pattern as primary with fallback to enumerated parallel reads when `atomic-agents bundle` is unavailable; on-demand recall pattern during conversation (INDEX → 1-3 atomic notes; wiki INDEX → specific pages) explicitly preserved per rule 6 (progressive disclosure). Test count: 2201 → **2244 passing** + 34 skipped (+43 net — 18 initial + 17 coverage-gap follow-up + 6 round-1 adversarial follow-up + 2 round-2 adversarial follow-up).

**Adversarial-review pre-merge fixes** (two Opus subagent rounds caught these — rule 11). Round 1 found 5 issues; round 2 verified the fixes AND caught 4 additional issues the round-1 commit introduced or missed — exactly the pattern rule 11 predicts:

*Round 1 fixes (commit 5 of 6):*

- **bundle.md write-protection threat documented** (232 filed for hardcoded enforcement). Spec/26 §"Trust model" expanded to call out that `bundle.md` is operator-authored config in the same class as `tools.md` / `judges.md` / `mandates.md` / `persona/*.md` — an agent with broad-enough write access could exfiltrate sensitive paths into its own next-turn context via `bundle.md`. v1 mitigation is documentation + the `_capture.enforce_write_path` convention (operators don't grant `<agent>/` in `write_paths`); hardcoded refusal tracked at 232.
- **Mtime staleness made strict (F-5).** Changed `<=` to `<` so a same-second edit-and-regenerate on 1s-granularity filesystems (ext4, HFS+) doesn't silently keep the old bundle. Also force regeneration when zero sources exist (previously `default=0.0` made empty-cascade always-fresh).
- **Deletion-aware staleness via `_staleness_paths` helper (F-9).** Previously, deleting a memory note / journal entry / policy file left the bundle stale forever (no source mtime was newer than the bundle). Now also tracks parent directory mtimes (memory/, wiki/, journal/, policy/) — POSIX bumps directory mtime on child add/remove, which catches deletions.
- **Encoding-tolerant rendering (F-8).** A new `_safe_read_text` helper falls back to `errors="replace"` with a per-file warning header so one non-UTF-8 byte in a single source file doesn't crash the whole bundle. Wraps the cascade resolver calls (`load_role_prompt`, `resolve_tools_md`, `load_project_layer`) so canon.md / style_guide.md / policy/* / PROMPT.md / tools.md / model.md degrade gracefully alongside persona files. `_iter_pinned` also catches `UnicodeDecodeError` per-file.
- **Pinned-frontmatter comment stripping (F-4).** `_iter_pinned`'s lexical scan now strips inline ` comment` suffixes from the `pinned:` value before the truthy-set membership check, so `pinned: true set by /pin-this` correctly pins (previously parsed as `value = "true set by /pin-this"` → not in truthy set → false negative).

*Round 2 fixes (commit 6 of 6) — caught what round 1 introduced or missed:*

- **`_safe_read_text` widened to catch OSError (R2-F4).** Round 1's commit message claimed "degrade gracefully" but the helper only caught `UnicodeDecodeError` — a chmod-000 source file (PermissionError) or IsADirectoryError still crashed the bundle. Now catches both, returns just a warning comment when the file is unreadable. The `_render_cascaded` cascade-resolver wrappers also widened from `except UnicodeDecodeError` to `except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError)` to match.
- **Cache file mode tightened to 0600 (R2-F9).** `_io.atomic_write` writes with the inheriting umask, which is 0644 on many shared hosts. After Round 1's F-1 spec update explicitly warned about secret-adjacent content in the bundle (operator extras can pull in identity-adjacent files), leaving the cache world-readable would have been the "docs say one thing, code does another" pattern rule 13 forbids. Now `os.chmod(bundle_path, 0o600)` runs after `atomic_write`; falls through silently on filesystems that don't support chmod (FAT32, some network mounts).

Zero behavior change for the runtime — none of these touch `AtomicAgent.load()` or `assemble_system_prompt()`. The bundle module is purely additive — removing the bundle CLI leaves the runtime unchanged. Filesystem-default deployments with no skill mode see zero new behavior. Zero behavior change for the runtime — `AtomicAgent.load()` does NOT consult the bundle; bundles are a skill-template optimization, not a runtime cache. Bundle module is purely additive — removing the bundle CLI leaves the runtime unchanged. Filesystem-default deployments with no skill mode see zero new behavior. Next: Muse's `~/.claude/skills/muse-showrunner-orb/SKILL.md` gets updated to use the new bundle-then-Read pattern, validating the 30-90s → 1-3s wall-time improvement end-to-end on the agent that motivated this Issue.

- **Mandate validation steps 7-9 ungated + reservation pattern + crash recovery + post-action verification events** ([124](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/124) — mandate primitive arc **PR 3b of 6**). Closes the load-bearing security property: cumulative token + external budget caps are now actually enforced (the PR 3a stub returned `mandate_budget_check_unavailable` BLOCK; this PR makes the checks real). Operators authoring `cumulative_external_usd: 6000` on a mandate now have that cap meaningfully defended against concurrent action races via the reservation pattern; crash-restart no longer silently loses budget tracking on real money; post-hoc divergence audits surface when an action's executed target differed from the authorization at proposal time. **Plan-subagent pre-implementation review** caught 3 amendments worth landing first (spec/29 commit `15089f2`): Risk 1 (cap_kind priority rule — adopted `monthly_external > daily_external > cumulative_external > monthly_token > daily_token > cumulative_token > per_action_max`; real-money first, the operator's most-authored window first); Risk 4 (`mandate_used` collapsed into the cost event as a derived view — `LogBackend.append(record)` is the framework's atomic primitive and adding a batch operation would amend a locked spec); Risk 7 (step 9 ESCALATE preempts step 8 BLOCK when both fire — operator-decision-required is the more nuanced surface than hard BLOCK). **Implementation:** 4 parallel Sonnet sub-agents on independent files completed in ~7 min wall-clock (vs ~3 hr sequential), one sub-agent wrote the 72-test suite; serial integration phase caught 3 real impl bugs (uncomputed projections under escalation-only mandates, wrong LogQuery filter in `_scan_orphan_reservations`, ActionClass.IRREVERSIBLE typo). **Two-round Opus adversarial review** (CLAUDE.md taste rule 11) caught additional findings: Round 1 found 2 P0s — agent_call cost event missing mandate_id (`_sum_prior_token_cost` would never match, cumulative_token_usd caps unenforceable) + reservations carrying `projected_usd=0.0` (compute_outstanding's stale-budget race defense vacuous); Round 2 found 3 P1/P2s — projection cache leak on BLOCK paths, replacement test tautological (re-implemented under-test logic inline), `mandate_cites_in_call` field impl-only without spec coverage. All fixed in commits `aec5404` (Round 1) and `2bc76a7` (Round 2). (1) **NEW** `atomic_agents/judge/mandate_reservations.py` (~720 LOC): `ReservationRecord` frozen dataclass; `MandateReservationManager` with create/commit/rollback/_expire/shutdown lifecycle, threading.Lock-serialized in-process state, threading.Timer-driven TTL watchers (in-process only per spec/29 Risk H — cross-process orphans route through `recover_orphan_reservations`); module-level `compute_outstanding(log_backend, scope, mandate_id)` implementing the 4-clause definition (no _committed/_rolled_back/_expired/_committed_on_recovery AND **no cost event with matching proposal_id** AND age < ttl_s) — clause 3 is the load-bearing addition that closes the cost-event-landed-without-_committed window; module-level `build_mandate_log_record_extras(successful_cites)` pure helper for agent_call mandate tagging (R2-2 extraction). (3) **`atomic_agents/mandate/types.py` + `atomic_agents/mandate/filesystem.py`** (+443 LOC): `MandateBackend.recover_orphan_reservations(log_backend, scope, *, lock_backend=None, lock_ttl_s=30)` Protocol method + capability flag `supports_crash_recovery`; FilesystemMandateBackend implementation with scan-inside-lock discipline per spec/29 Risk 3 (LockBackend lease serializes both the orphan scan AND the recovery emission so concurrent recoverers can't double-emit); token orphans → `mandate_reservation_committed_on_recovery` at projected_usd (pessimistic over-report — operator over-billed once, recoverable via reconcile CLI vs silent under-billing); external orphans → BOTH `_committed_on_recovery` AND `mandate_reservation_external_unverified` (operator-facing doctor surface for "did the external action actually run? verify in Stripe/vendor"). - **Pre-124-PR-3b scaffolding — `CostEstimatorRegistry` per-agent named registry + `ToolDefinition.cost_estimator_id` / `expected_external_cost_usd` fields + fail-fast validation at `tool_registry.register()` time + `PRIMITIVE_MANDATE_RESERVATION` log primitive + spec/29 amendments for 2 SEVERE + 4 HIGH plan-subagent risks** (124 PR 3b prep — no separate issue; lands ahead of the budget-projection + reservation-pattern body PR). Plan-subagent pass before PR 3b identified 2 SEVERE risks (cost projection's `Callable` field on `ToolDefinition` can't satisfy spec/25 MUST 4 Tier B round-trip — same shape as the target_extractor Callable risk that re-architecture-cycled PR 3a prep 218; outstanding-reservation definition gap that lets sequential proposals double-count) + 4 HIGH risks (multi-process crash-recovery double-counting without LockBackend serialization; token-cost projection baseline drift; cap-overshoot precision bound undefined; cap-priority resolution ambiguity when multiple caps fire simultaneously) + 1 missing post-action verification event family. This prep PR resolves them all in advance so PR 3b's body is purely budget-projection + reservation-pattern logic, not scaffolding + re-architecture. (1) **`atomic_agents/judge/cost_estimator_registry.py`** (~200 LOC) — new `CostEstimatorRegistry` mirroring `TargetExtractorRegistry` shape exactly: per-agent in-memory `dict[str, Callable[[dict], float]]`; `register(name, callable)` rejects collisions + non-alphanumeric/underscore name characters; `replace()` for explicit overwrite path; `has(name)` membership check; `estimate(tool_name, tool_arguments, estimator_id=None)` returns `float('inf')` when (estimator missing, estimator raises any exception, estimator returns non-numeric) — fail-closed per spec/29 line 380 so caller (`MandateCheck` step 8) emits `mandate_external_cost_unprojectable` BLOCK; named estimator missing on `tool_registry.register()` raises `UnknownCostEstimator` (loud failure at register time per spec/29 §"Registration order discipline" so operator misconfiguration surfaces BEFORE first mandate-citing dispatch — same shape + same discipline as `UnknownTargetExtractor` from PR 3a). **No built-in estimators ship by default** — unlike target extractors (where heuristic field-name fallback is sensible for the common case), external-cost projection is so tool-specific that "guess from arg shape" is the wrong default; tools with dynamic pricing register an explicit estimator at agent setup, tools with static pricing use the new `expected_external_cost_usd` field, mandates with `*_external_usd` caps over tools with neither registered fail-closed at evaluation time. (2) **`atomic_agents/tools.py`** — `ToolDefinition` gets two new optional fields: `cost_estimator_id: str | None = None` (names the registered estimator) and `expected_external_cost_usd: float | None = None` (static-pricing alternative for tools with deterministic per-call cost). `ToolRegistry.register()` signature extends with `cost_estimator_registry: CostEstimatorRegistry | None = None` kwarg; when both the kwarg is passed AND the tool's `cost_estimator_id` is set, validates the ID is registered or raises `UnknownCostEstimator` immediately (mirrors the existing `target_extractor_id` validation block byte-for-byte in shape). (3) **`atomic_agents/agent.py`** wiring — `self._cost_estimators = CostEstimatorRegistry()` initialized BEFORE tool_registry loading; new `register_cost_estimator(name, callable)` public method (per-agent scoping — coordinator estimators do NOT flow to delegates per spec/15 delegate isolation + spec/25 Decision 9); both `self.tool_registry.register(...)` call sites in the descriptor-loading loop now pass `cost_estimator_registry=self._cost_estimators` alongside the existing `target_extractor_registry=self._target_extractors` kwarg so the fail-fast validation actually fires during agent construction. (4) **`atomic_agents/logs/types.py`** — new `PRIMITIVE_MANDATE_RESERVATION = "mandate_reservation"` log primitive constant for the reservation-event family PR 3b introduces (`mandate_reservation_created` / `mandate_reservation_committed` / `mandate_reservation_rolled_back` / `mandate_reservation_expired`); landed in prep so PR 3b's body doesn't ship a new constant alongside the reservation logic. (5) **spec/29 amendments** resolving the 6 plan-subagent risks + 1 event-family gap: Risk A (outstanding-reservation definition — explicit "outstanding = created AND NOT (committed OR rolled_back OR expired)" with `proposal_id` pairing rule so two sequential MandateCheck calls on the same in-flight proposal don't double-reserve); Risk E (atomic emission discipline — `mandate_used` event MUST emit atomically with the cost path's reservation commit, not on a separate code path that can crash between); Risk B (crash recovery serialization — multi-process recovery MUST acquire a `LockBackend.acquire(scope='mandate-recovery:<scope>')` lease before reconciling orphan reservations; without it, two processes recovering the same orphan reservation under pessimistic "over-report > under-report" semantics double-charge); Risk H (TTL expiry clarification — reservation TTL expiry is IN-PROCESS-ONLY; cross-process crash recovery routes through LockBackend reconciliation, not TTL — closes the cross-process double-expire window); Risk F (token-cost projection baseline pinned to "immediately preceding iteration" + cap-overshoot bound — projection MAY overshoot the cap by `≤ 1 × (max_observed_iteration_cost_usd × 1.5)` before falling back to fail-closed BLOCK); Risk G (cap-priority resolution — `mandate_cap_exceeded_block` event extended with `additional_caps_exceeded: list[str]` field listing all caps simultaneously exceeded + explicit cap_kind priority rule when multiple fire: `monthly_external_usd > daily_external_usd > monthly_token_usd > daily_token_usd > per_action_max_usd` so operators see the primary trigger first); Risk I + event-family gap (3 new lifecycle events for post-action verification: `mandate_action_verified` when the post-action outcome matches the cited mandate's permitted scope, `mandate_action_diverged` when the action's actual effect exceeded what was authorized — operator-facing audit signal, NOT a refund mechanism in this version, and `mandate_action_verification_unavailable` when the action class doesn't support post-hoc verification — emitted exactly once per `external_side_effect` or `irreversible` action so dashboards can count verifications without inflation). Spec/29 §"Validation steps" step 8 prose rewritten around the named-registry shape (was: `cost_estimator: Callable`); spec/29 §"Cost reservation pattern" and §"Crash recovery for reservations" now reflect the LockBackend serialization + TTL clarification + outstanding-reservation pairing rule. (6) **`tests/test_mandate_check.py`** — new `TestCostEstimatorRegistry` class (10 tests) mirroring `TestTargetExtractorRegistry`: empty-at-construction (no built-ins), collision-refusal, invalid-name-character rejection, replace-overwrites, estimate-with-args, unknown-id-raises, none-id-returns-inf, exception-returns-inf, non-numeric-return-returns-inf, int-coerced-to-float; new `TestToolRegistryCostEstimatorValidation` class (4 tests) pinning the fail-fast register-time validation: unknown id raises `UnknownCostEstimator`, registered id succeeds, no-estimator-no-registry succeeds (back-compat for tools that don't declare a cost_estimator_id), `expected_external_cost_usd` field round-trips through `ToolRegistry.get`; new `test_atomic_agent_register_cost_estimator_public_api` (1 test) pinning the `agent.register_cost_estimator()` public surface mirrors `agent.register_target_extractor`. Filesystem-default deployments with no `mandates.md` files AND no `cost_estimator_id` declared on any `ToolDefinition` see ZERO behavior change end-to-end — the registry is constructed empty, the new fields default to None, the validation block is a no-op. The new module establishes the operator-facing surface that PRs 2-5 build on. New package `atomic_agents/mandate/` follows the 7-backend established pattern (Memory / LLM / Judge / Lock / Log / AgentProfile / ToolRegistry): `types.py` (canonical dataclasses `Mandate`, `MandateConstraints`, `TargetPattern`, `TimeWindow`, `ProjectMandateMeta`, `MandateCapabilities`, `RevocationState`/`ActionClass` enums, exception hierarchy `MandateError` / `MandateInvalid` / `MandateNotFound` / `MandateStateSchemaUnsupported`), `backend.py` (`runtime_checkable` `MandateBackend` Protocol + `register_mandate_backend` / `get_mandate_backend` / `list_mandate_backends` / `get_default_mandate_backend` honoring `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MANDATE_BACKEND` env var), `filesystem.py` (`FilesystemMandateBackend(scope_root)` reference impl with path-traversal refusal at the API boundary per spec/29 MUST #1, per-scope isolation enforced at the storage layer per MUST 2, atomic state writes via `_io.atomic_write` per MUST 3, source-hash recomputation on every `load_mandate` per MUST 4, derived-EXPIRED state computed at load time per spec/29 line 548, lexicographic ordering of `list_mandates` mirroring tool registry convention; URL factory `make_filesystem_mandate_backend_from_url("filesystem:///path")`). New top-level `atomic_agents/mandates_md.py` parser: parses per-section markdown with embedded YAML matching the `judges_md.py` convention; enforces mandate ID regex `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*` (≤64 chars) per spec/29 line 298; refuses duplicate IDs within a file; enforces constraint enforceability discipline (mandates without enforceable constraints AND without `unconstrained: true` + non-empty justification are rejected per spec/29 §"Constraint enforceability"); parses `_meta` section discipline (project-root files honor `per_agent_mandate_policy: open|listed|forbidden`; per-agent files with `_meta` log a warning + skip — `check_mandate_meta_misplaced` will surface in PR 4); computes `sha256:` algorithm-prefixed source hash on the canonical section bytes. Parametrized conformance suite (`tests/test_mandate_protocol_conformance.py`): 42 conformance test functions + 10 parametrized bad-id cases run across registered backends — each test pins a spec/29 MUST (path-traversal refusal, scope isolation, state-via-Protocol-methods, source-hash recomputation, list includes revoked/expired, MandateNotFound vs MandateInvalid, schema_version raises, capability honesty). `tests/test_mandate_filesystem_backend.py`: 20 filesystem-specific tests covering constructor refusals, disk layout discipline (`<scope_root>/<name>/mandates.md` for agent scope vs `<scope_root>/mandates.md` for project scope), `.judge-state/mandates.json` placement, URL factory parsing. **Zero behavior change** end-to-end — `AtomicAgent.__init__` signature unchanged; the new module is registered at framework import via `register_mandate_backend("filesystem", FilesystemMandateBackend)` but not consumed (PR 2 wires through `AtomicAgent.__init__` + all 4 runners + `doctor.check_mandate_backend`). All existing 1961 tests unaffected. Test count: 1961 → **2032 passing** + 34 skipped (+71 net: 42 conformance + 20 filesystem-specific + 9 helper / registry-resolution tests). Depends on spec/29 amendments shipped in 213 (Implementer contract section + named target_extractor registry + state-via-Protocol-methods + schema_version discipline + suspicious-rebind throttle + forever-stable BLOCK reasons + reservation IDs in cap-exceeded events + step-9 split-line); rebase landed against post-213 main.

Fixed

- **Per-call dedup for tool-allowlist denial emissions** ([273](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/273) — PolicyBackend arc PR 4 follow-up). In log-only mode, the LLM doesn't see refusals and may re-attempt a denied tool every iteration of the multi-turn loop; without dedup the audit log records N events per denied tool per call. PR 4 tracks emitted denials in a per-call `set[str]` reset at `agent.call()` entry; emission skips when the tool name is already in the set. The audit shape stays uniform across log-only and enforce modes; in enforce mode the synthesized `policy_blocked` `ToolCallResult` naturally bounds re-attempts via LLM feedback, but the dedup ensures the same shape if log-only is re-enabled. MCP discovery already emits at most once per server per call by construction (single discovery loop), so no equivalent set is needed there. New `tests/test_policy_noncap_integration.py::test_repeated_denied_tool_emits_once_per_call` + `::test_different_denied_tools_each_emit_once` regression pin.

- **Caldwell sample JSONL now matches the shipped audit-trail shape** ([137](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/137)). The sample log records in `docs/samples/caldwell/log/2026-05/*.jsonl` now include `run_id`, helper child rows resolve `parent_run_id` back to an in-sample run, and `docs/spec/09-cost-observability.md` names `run_id` as a minimum log field. The sibling JSONL example in `docs/spec/01-anatomy.md` was aligned in the same pass so the two anatomy/observability spec docs no longer contradict each other on minimum log fields. A docs-consistency regression test pins the sample shape so future curated samples do not drift from `AtomicAgent._log()`.

- **`SQLiteToolRegistryBackend` cold-start WAL race — same shape as 208, now closed for the tool registry too** ([215](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/215)). The #208 fix landed for `atomic_agents/logs/sqlite.py` last cycle (busy_timeout PRAGMA + 7-attempt retry on `SQLITE_BUSY`/`SQLITE_LOCKED`). `atomic_agents/registry/sqlite.py` had the busy_timeout PRAGMA from 64 PR 3 but was missing the retry-on-locked loop — the same defense gap. Surfaced as a CI Python 3.11 flake on PR 214: `tests/test_tool_registry_sqlite_backend.py::test_concurrent_schema_init_no_race` failed with `database is locked` on the WAL transition (PR 214 doesn't touch registry/ — pre-existing flake exposed). `registry/sqlite.py:_get_conn` now wraps the WAL pragma in the same 7-attempt retry loop matching `exc.sqlite_errorcode in (SQLITE_BUSY, SQLITE_LOCKED)` (Python 3.11+) with exponential backoff (~3.15s cumulative sleep, plus up to 5s busy_timeout per attempt). Pre-fix: existing concurrent-schema-init test passed locally on macOS but CI 3.11 flaked. Post-fix: same test passes 10/10 consecutive runs locally; new regression tests pin both the primitive (`test_busy_timeout_set_before_wal_pragma` — `PRAGMA busy_timeout` returns 5000ms on a fresh connection) and the end-to-end behavior (`test_concurrent_schema_init_no_race_under_repeated_runs` — 10 fresh dbs × 16 concurrent threads). Spec/25 §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" MUST 5 extended with the third lesson (mirrors spec/22 MUST 4 from 208): SQLite-shape backends MUST set busy_timeout before WAL AND retry the WAL transition on SQLITE_BUSY/SQLITE_LOCKED. Implementation now meets its own spec. Filesystem-default deployments unaffected — only operators pinning `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND=sqlite` saw the flake (and only at high concurrency). No operator-visible behavior change beyond reliability.

New top-level doc telling contributors what to expect when they open a PR: a four-tier risk classification (trivial docs → security-sensitive code → stranger + larger change), the specific signals that auto-elevate any PR's tier (dependency changes to `pyproject.toml` / `uv.lock`, `.github/workflows/` edits, binary additions, PR description not matching diff scope, disabling or removing existing tests, brand-new GitHub account + non-trivial change), how to make a PR easy to merge (pick from `good first issue` / `help wanted`, keep scope focused, write a description that matches the diff, open an issue first when touching design-rule territory or adding dependencies), what review tooling is run (pre-landing diff analysis, independent cross-model review for security-sensitive changes, OWASP-style dependency + secret scans), and realistic response-time expectations for an alpha single-maintainer project. The doc references `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, and `SECURITY.md` so contributors can navigate to the deeper community-health set. Pre-existing review discipline was implicit per CLAUDE.md rule 11 ("Adversarial review in rounds, not passes"); `MAINTAINER.md` makes the process explicit at the surface so contributors don't have to read the codebase to figure out what scrutiny their PR will face.

- **spec/29 mandates — pre-implementation amendments before 124 PR 1 opens** (no issue — recorded under spec/29 RFC convention; spec stays RFC until 124 PR 5 locks it). Six structural amendments landed via /office-hours 2026-05-17 + /plan-eng-review 2026-05-17: (1) New §"Implementer contract for mandate backends" section adds the `MandateBackend` Protocol surface with 8 normative MUSTs (path-traversal refusal, per-scope isolation, state-via-Protocol-methods, source-hash recomputation per load, lifecycle events through LogBackend, base reservation event discriminator shape, pessimistic crash recovery, capability honesty) — mirrors spec/22/24/25 Implementer contract patterns. `FilesystemMandateBackend` is the only reference impl in v1; future SaaS / mobile / Slack-bot adapters slot in via `register_mandate_backend(...)` without forking core. (2) §"Target extraction" rewritten — `target_extractor` is now a named per-agent registry (`agent.register_target_extractor(name, callable)`) with built-in heuristics pre-registered per-agent; `ToolDefinition.target_extractor_id: str | None` references the registry by string ID. The earlier `Callable` shape was rejected because `Callable` fields cannot satisfy spec/25 MUST 4 Tier B's lossless round-trip obligation (which we just pinned with conformance tests in PR 211). Per-agent scoping matches spec/25 Decision 9 + spec/15 delegate-isolation precedent. (3) §"Lifecycle event deduplication" updated — state persistence is a `MandateBackend.read_state(scope) / write_state(scope, state)` Protocol contract, NOT a filesystem-path contract. `FilesystemMandateBackend` persists at `<scope_root>/.judge-state/mandates.json` (the prior path); future backends use SQL tables / API stores. State shape now carries `schema_version: 1` from PR 3a onward; unknown versions raise `MandateStateSchemaUnsupported`. (4) New §"Suspicious-rebind throttle" closes the source-hash-before-state edit window — when `MandateCheck` surfaces `mandate_state_inconsistent`, the framework refuses re-binding on the same mandate for 60s default (`judges.md Mandates / suspicious_rebind_throttle_s`). New lifecycle event `mandate_rebind_suspicious_throttled`; new doctor check `check_mandate_rebind_throttle_active`. Closes a prompt-injection / hallucinating-actor exploitation window without requiring filesystem locks. (5) New §"BLOCK reason naming discipline" — `BLOCK` reasons emitted by `MandateCheck` MUST be forever-stable strings (no PR identifiers / version markers / phase suffixes leak into JSONL audit reasons). Temporary-cause stories belong in CHANGELOG entries, not reason fields. Applied immediately: PR 3a's stubbed budget reason is `mandate_budget_check_unavailable`, NOT `_in_3a`. (6) `mandate_cap_exceeded_block` lifecycle event extended with `contributing_reservation_ids` array + `reconcile_cli_hint` string — operators see WHY they're blocked (which orphan reservations pushed cumulative over) and WHAT to run (`atomic-agents mandate reconcile <id> --action ...`), not just THAT they are. Cross-link from §"Crash recovery" → §"Audit shape" makes the surfacing path explicit. (7) New §"Validation step split between PR 3a and PR 3b" documents the implementation discipline locked at /plan-eng-review — PR 3a ships steps 1-6 + stub 7-9; PR 3b ungates 7-9. Step 9 (escalation thresholds) explicitly belongs in PR 3b because it operates on projected costs from steps 7+8 (which are stubbed in 3a). Status banner gains a "Pre-implementation amendments" subsection listing all six. Zero behavior change — this is spec-only prep before the implementation PRs (124 PR 1 of 6) open. Test count unchanged at 1961.

Fixed

- **`SQLiteLogBackend` cold-start WAL race — concurrent thread/process appends to a fresh db no longer flake** ([208](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/208)). `atomic_agents/logs/sqlite.py:_get_conn` now sets `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` BEFORE `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` and wraps the WAL transition in a 7-attempt retry loop that matches on `sqlite_errorcode in (SQLITE_BUSY, SQLITE_LOCKED)` (Python 3.11+) with exponential backoff (0.05/0.1/0.2/0.4/0.8/1.6s cumulative ~3.15s of sleep, plus up to 5s `busy_timeout` per attempt). Same shape as the parallel #64 PR 3 fix in `atomic_agents/registry/sqlite.py`: when N threads/processes race the very first WAL transition on a fresh db file, `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` raises `sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked` because the WAL transition needs an EXCLUSIVE lock and SQLite's journal-mode-switch path is not fully covered by the busy_handler. The busy_timeout PRAGMA alone is insufficient under heavy contention (empirically reproduced at ~65% fail rate locally on macOS even after the busy_timeout was added — verified during this fix); the additional retry loop closes the gap. Matching on `sqlite_errorcode` (not message text) means a future SQLite wording change can't silently swallow legitimate `SQLITE_CORRUPT` / `SQLITE_READONLY` errors. Pre-existing `tests/test_log_sqlite_backend.py::test_concurrent_appends_from_threads` macOS flake (~80% local fail rate per the 64 arc handoff) now passes 20/20 consecutive runs. New regression tests pin both the primitive (`test_busy_timeout_set_before_wal_pragma` — `PRAGMA busy_timeout` returns 5000ms on a fresh connection) and the end-to-end concurrent behavior (`test_concurrent_appends_no_wal_race_under_repeated_runs` — 10 fresh dbs × 4 threads × 10 appends each, all 10 iterations succeed). Spec/22 §"Implementer contract for queryable backends" MUST 4 extended to require `busy_timeout` before WAL + retry on `SQLITE_BUSY`/`SQLITE_LOCKED` for SQLite-shape backends (parity with spec/25 §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" MUST 5; the SQLiteLogBackend implementation now meets its own spec). Filesystem-default deployments unaffected — only operators pinning `ATOMIC_AGENTS_LOG_BACKEND=sqlite` for indexed query/aggregate/retention saw the flake. No operator-visible behavior change beyond reliability.

Added

- **`ToolRegistryBackend` Tier B conformance pins — `input_schema` / `description` / `handler` round-trip enforced for structured-storage backends** ([207](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/207)). Spec/25 §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" MUST #4 Tier B is normative — structured-storage backends MUST round-trip every `ToolDefinition` field that affects dispatch losslessly. Before this PR, the parametrized conformance suite pinned `name`, `classification`, and `version` round-trip but NOT `input_schema` / `description` / `handler` — a future Tier B backend (PyPI, company-internal HTTP, SaaS-database) could silently drop or normalize those fields and pass green. Three new parametrized conformance tests in `tests/test_tool_registry_protocol_conformance.py` close the gap: `test_load_tool_round_trips_input_schema` asserts strict dict equality on a non-trivial schema (nested objects + integer constraints `minimum`/`maximum`/`default` + booleans `additionalProperties: false` + required arrays); `test_load_tool_round_trips_description` asserts exact-string round-trip with quotes + unicode (catches whitespace normalization, quote coercion, escape rewriting); `test_load_tool_round_trips_handler_callable` invokes the materialized handler with structured input exercising real branches (`msg` + `count` + nested `options.upper`) and asserts the return value matches source semantics (catches a stub or no-op substitution). Both reference backends (`FilesystemToolRegistryBackend` + `SQLiteToolRegistryBackend`) pass without code changes — the tests are pure regression pins for future backends. Test counts: +3 functions × 2 backends = +6 invocations; no skips. Spec/25 MUST 4 footnote updated from "Closing that gap is tracked in 207" to "closed by [207](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/207) ... via three parametrized conformance tests". Filed inline during the #64 PR 4 round-2 Step 11 adversarial — the new tests are the "regression-prevention for future PyPI/HTTP backends" the issue body called for.

- **`ToolRegistryBackend` Protocol locked + `Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends` documented + README/CLAUDE.md status refresh** ([64](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/64) — tool-registry arc **PR 4 of 4; closes the #64 arc**). `docs/spec/25-tool-registry-backend.md` status flips from DRAFT to **LOCKED** — the spec now matches the shipped implementation across all four arc PRs. New §"Implementer contract for registry-backed tool backends" (8 normative MUSTs) consolidates the registry-backend pattern that PRs 1–3 introduced into a single section so future PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP / SaaS-database contributors have one place to read the obligations: path-traversal refusal for operator-supplied `name` AND `agent_scope` at the API boundary (PR 1 Step 11 adversarial defenses); cross-scope isolation enforced at the storage layer (`WHERE agent_scope = ?` on every query for SQL backends; per-agent `<agent>/tools/` natural scoping for filesystem); `install()` atomicity via **INSERT-first + atomic_write-on-success-only** ordering (PR 3 Step 11 P1 REPRODUCED 50/50 TOCTOU race fix — the original handler-atomic_write-first order caused concurrent installs to destroy the winner's handler file via the loser's rollback `unlink()`); two-tier descriptor round-trip (Tier A raw-text-preserving for filesystem-shape backends; Tier B lossy-parse-documented for structured-storage backends like SQLite that store `descriptor_json` + handler `.py` on disk — operator notes / YAML comments NOT preserved on Tier B; both tiers round-trip every `ToolDefinition` dispatch field losslessly); idempotent schema initialization + `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` BEFORE `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` for SQLite-shape backends (PR 3 Step 11 P1 REPRODUCED 3/5 multi-process WAL race fix — same shape as the pre-existing `test_log_sqlite_backend.py::test_concurrent_appends_from_threads` flake queued as a one-line follow-up); capability honesty (claim-vs-behavior parity enforced by the parametrized conformance suite — `test_capability_parity_install` round-trip pin from PR 3 Step 11 testing CRITICAL fix); trust-model framing for shared-catalog backends (the catalog is process-shared but trust is NOT — multi-tenant deployments MUST scope at the process level, one framework process per tenant; the judge layer is the runtime safety net, not the registry layer); connection / handler lifecycle safe under thread-life-tied cleanup (`threading.local` for pool-bearing backends; `check_same_thread=True` for `:memory:`; per-instance `tempfile.mkdtemp()` for `handlers_root` honoring the non-persistent promise; `handlers_root` refuses `<= 1`-component paths). The parametrized conformance suite (`tests/test_tool_registry_protocol_conformance.py`) runs against every registered backend so the contract is verified by the same tests that pin `list_tools` / `load_tool` / `install` / `uninstall` / `validate` / `capabilities` semantics — no separate "registry-backend" test suite required.

- **`SQLiteToolRegistryBackend` reference impl + parametrized conformance over both backends + `install` / `uninstall` flipped True** ([64](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/64) — tool-registry arc **PR 3 of 4**). Operators can now pin `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND=sqlite` (+ optional `ATOMIC_AGENTS_TOOL_REGISTRY_BACKEND_URL=sqlite:///path/to/tools.db?agent_scope=<name>`) to back the tool catalog on a single SQLite file instead of per-agent `<agent>/tools/`. Without the URL env var, defaults to `<agent_root>/.tools.db` with `agent_scope=<agent_root.name>` so single-host operators get a working SQLite default by flipping ONE env var. Stdlib `sqlite3`, no optional dependency. **SaaS-shaped tool catalogs are now one Protocol implementation away** — the eventual PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP backends slot in via the same `register_tool_registry_backend("<id>", cls)` API. Hybrid storage shape (plan-subagent Risk A fix): SQLite stores metadata only (descriptor JSON + handler path + version + classification + scope + timestamps); handler bodies live on disk under `<handlers_root>/<agent_scope>/<name>.py` for inspectability + closures-over-module-globals + module-level imports + `session = requests.Session()` patterns at handler-module top-level — base64-exec'd-source was rejected at the plan-subagent stage because it silently breaks all of these. Schema: `tools(agent_scope, name, descriptor_json, handler_path, version, classification, created_at, updated_at, PRIMARY KEY (agent_scope, name))` — composite PK so two scopes can both have a tool named the same. `meta(key PK, value)` schema-version tracking with idempotent `INSERT OR IGNORE` cold-start race fix (#61 PR 3 + 63 PR 3 lesson). Cross-scope isolation enforced at every query (`list_tools` / `load_tool` / `uninstall` ALL filter `WHERE agent_scope = ?`); the scope is hardcoded from the constructor and refused at construction for path-traversal tokens (defense-in-depth on top of SQL parametrization). `install()` validates descriptor + imports handler at install time (rejects malformed YAML, missing handler symbol, non-callable handler, descriptor name-mismatch — operators don't ship broken tools that fail at every agent construction). `uninstall()` is idempotent. Capabilities: `supports_install=True, supports_uninstall=True, supports_versioning=False (column round-trips for forward compat but PR 3 rejects non-None version at install — capability honesty per plan-subagent Risk L), supports_sandbox_validate=False, supports_skills_catalog=False, durable=True (False for :memory:)`. URL factory `make_sqlite_tool_registry_backend_from_url` honors `sqlite:///path?agent_scope=<name>`; refuses non-sqlite scheme, netloc, fragments, duplicate query params, multi-value scope, unknown query params (silent drops are operator-intent footguns). `:memory:` mode is single-threaded test-only (check_same_thread=True; ProgrammingError at first cross-thread use rather than silent corruption); handlers_root defaults to a per-instance tempdir to honor the non-persistent promise.

**Plan-subagent caught 3 SEVERE risks pre-implementation** (saved a re-architecture cycle): Risk A (base64-exec handler storage breaks closures + module imports + top-level setup) → switched to filepath storage using the same `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` path the filesystem reference uses; Risk D (schema PK `name` alone can't work multi-scope) → composite `PRIMARY KEY (agent_scope, name)` from the start; Risk J (conformance suite needs `make_tool_in_backend` helper before SQLite factory lands — same shape as 63 PR 3's `make_agent_in_backend`) → added pre-implementation. Risks B, K, L, F, G, E all documented + addressed in spec/25.

**Step 11 adversarial (Opus subagent — Codex skipped per the standing rule) caught 3 P1 REPRODUCED + 2 P2 + 3 critical testing gaps**, all fixed pre-merge:
- **P1 TOCTOU race in `install()` REPRODUCED 50/50 pre-fix** — original order (atomic_write handler → INSERT) caused concurrent installs to destroy the winner's handler file via the loser's rollback `unlink()`. Fix: reordered to INSERT-first + atomic_write-on-success-only; loser sees `rowcount=0` and raises `ToolAlreadyInstalled` WITHOUT touching disk. Regression test pins the invariant (concurrent install race with assertion that winner's handler file survives + remains importable).
- **P1 multi-process WAL race REPRODUCED 3/5** — `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` raised `database is locked` when N processes raced fresh-db open. Fix: `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` before WAL pragma so concurrent WAL-transition contention waits instead of failing.
- **P1 URL factory credential leak REPRODUCED** — `make_sqlite_tool_registry_backend_from_url('postgres://user:secrethost/db')` echoed the raw URL with credentials in its `ValueError` messages, propagating to any caller of `get_default_tool_registry_backend` → `AtomicAgent.__init__`. Fix: new `_redact_url` helper applied to all 5 ValueError sites in the URL factory.
- **P2 :memory: handlers_root wrote to disk** — defaulted to `Path.cwd() / .handlers/`, contradicting the "non-persistent / test-only" docstring promise + letting two :memory: instances clobber each other's on-disk handlers. Fix: per-instance `tempfile.mkdtemp()` default.
- **P2 handlers_root accepted `/`** — constructor took any path without validation; running as root on misconfigured Linux would write to `/<scope>/`. Fix: refuse paths with `<= 1` component after resolution.
- **Testing CRITICAL**: `test_capability_parity_install` was a tautology (only checked `NotImplementedError` not raised; swallowed all other exceptions via `except Exception: pass`). Fix: rewritten as a real install + verify-in-list_tools round-trip — catches "backend whose install() silently no-ops" — exactly plan-subagent's Risk C.
- **Testing CRITICAL**: install() previously accepted non-callable `handler = 42` (only `validate()` caught it). Fix: `_import_handler` now raises `ToolHandlerImportFailed` on non-callable too — install refuses up-front (filesystem reference inherits the strengthened check; the docstring-promise that install validates handlers is now honest).

Test suite goes 1825 → 1922 passing (+97 net: 44 SQLite-specific + ~45 parametrized conformance now running ×2 backends − 8 capability-gated skips on filesystem + 8 Step 11 regression tests for the new defenses). The parametrized conformance suite is the load-bearing artifact — future PyPI / git / company-internal-HTTP backends drop into `BACKEND_FACTORIES` and inherit ~37 conformance tests.
