- **`LockBackend` Protocol scaffolding** ([60](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/60) — lock layer arc PR 1 of 4). The `LockBackend` Protocol contract, canonical dataclasses (`LockHandle`, `LockCapabilities`), `FilesystemLockBackend` reference implementation wrapping today's `fcntl.flock` semantics, exception alias (`LockBusy` as the new canonical name with `AgentLockBusy = LockBusy` preserved for backwards compat), and registry primitives ship today under `atomic_agents.locks`. **No behavior change** — `_locks.AgentLock` is unchanged; the four legacy lock sites (`agent.py:2073`, `dream.py:1098`, `memory/filesystem.py:506`, the inline `_fcntl.flock` at `memory/filesystem.py:629-639`) plus `doctor.check_locks` at `doctor.py:725-790` (the diagnostic that probes ``.lock`` via raw ``fcntl`` and surfaces "another process holds this agent's lock" to operators) continue to use the legacy primitives. PR 2 of the arc routes all five sites through the backend (with `doctor.check_locks` going through `backend.is_held()`) and converts `_locks.py` into a deprecation shim per CLAUDE.md rule #14; PR 3 ships a distributed reference impl (Redis or tested mock); PR 4 locks `docs/spec/21-lock-backend.md` and parameterizes the conformance suite across both backends. Protocol surface: `acquire(name, timeout) -> LockHandle`, `release(handle)` (idempotent), `renew(handle) -> bool` (no-op-returns-True for non-lease backends; reserved in PR 1 so PR 2's long-running operations — `agent.call()` 5-30 min, `dream.start()` 10+ min — can install a heartbeat without retroactive Protocol extension), `is_held(name)` (diagnostic only, racy by design), `capabilities()`. Scope is bound at backend construction (not per call) — `FilesystemLockBackend(scope_root)` ties the backend to a directory; `acquire(name="")` looks up `<scope>/.lock` (preserves legacy on-disk artifact so doctor and external scripts keep working), `acquire(name="dream")` looks up `<scope>/.dream.lock`. Reentrancy is NON-REENTRANT by default — a second `acquire()` of the same name from the same process raises `LockBusy` unless the backend advertises `supports_reentrancy=True`. Capabilities advertised honestly: filesystem is `single_host_only=True`, `supports_reentrancy=False`, `supports_lease=False`. New `docs/spec/21-lock-backend.md` (DRAFT — locks at PR 4) documents the prose contract mirroring spec/20's shape; the `is_held` racy-by-design property, the `_per_file_lock` deliberate-non-subsumption rationale, the operator-surface decision (registration-only, no `locks.md` — lock backend is deployment-level, not agent-author-level), and the reserved `AsyncLockBackend` / `ShareableLockBackend` future namespaces are all called out. 29 new tests: 17 in `tests/test_lock_protocol_conformance.py` (parameterized via a `backend_factory` fixture ready to receive PR 3's distributed reference impl — covers Protocol surface, backend_id stability, acquire/release/renew/is_held/capabilities semantics, non-reentrant contract with capability-parity assertion, named lock isolation, timeout fail-fast / deadline / grant-after-release, context-manager release-on-exception, `LockBusy` subclassing `AtomicAgentsError`, `AgentLockBusy is LockBusy` identity); 12 in `tests/test_lock_filesystem_backend.py` (filesystem-specific: `backend_id == "filesystem"`, empty-name → `.lock` mapping, named → `.<name>.lock`, PID-file format parity with legacy `AgentLock`, scope_root auto-create, str-path constructor, custom `poll_interval`, kernel-level crash recovery on holder death, `is_held` honest after holder process death, registry resolves `"filesystem"` → `FilesystemLockBackend`, doctor-compatibility same-process `fcntl.flock` probe pins the `acquire("")` → `<scope>/.lock` contract that doctor's `check_locks` reads, F1-regression test ensures re-entering a released handle raises rather than silently no-op'ing into a phantom critical section). Total: 1327 → 1356 (+29 net). Plan review (Opus subagent, per the 112-arc reviewer-roster rule — Codex skipped) pinned three architectural commitments before any code was written: constructor-bound scope (P0-1), `name=""` → `.lock` on-disk-preserving mapping (P0-2), `renew()` in PR 1 not PR 3 (P1-3 — avoids the Protocol-extension trap PR 3c hit in the 112 arc). Step 11 adversarial (Opus subagent) then caught F1 (silent phantom critical section on handle-reuse-after-release — violated CLAUDE.md rule 8), F3 (`backend_id` shape inconsistency — was a class attribute, now a `property` matching the LLM module's `provider_id` pattern), F5 (no test pinned the `acquire("")`/doctor compatibility contract — now pinned by a same-process raw-`fcntl.flock` probe test), and bumped the multiprocessing sentinel from 3s to 10s for CI flakiness safety margin.

- **Strict JSON-Schema validation of amended `tool_arguments` via opt-in `[validation]` extra** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 5b of 5b; closes the second deferred item from PR 4's CHANGELOG and concludes the #112 arc-with-amendments).

`judges.md` gains a top-level `validation:` field gating how the framework validates amended `tool_arguments` on REVISE before executing the bound action. Two values ship: `weakened` (default; PR 3c behavior — tool registered + dict shape + canonical `arguments_hash` recompute) and `strict` (opt-in; runs `jsonschema.validate(args, registered.input_schema)` after the weakened checks). The new `[validation]` extra adds `jsonschema>=4.0` as an optional dependency.

Operators upgrading see no behavior change — `validation` default-fills to `weakened` for any `judges.md` that omits the field, and existing deployments without `judges.md` continue to skip the judge layer entirely per the PR 2a opt-in gate. To enable strict: install `pip install 'atomic-agents-stack[validation]'` (or `uv sync --extra validation`), then set `validation: strict` in `judges.md`. The install order is enforced — the parser probes `import jsonschema` at agent-load when strict is configured and raises `JudgePolicyInvalid` LOUD if the package isn't importable. Operators flipping strict without first installing the extra never see the failure delayed to the first amendment.

**The gate is on the parsed config, not on transitive import availability** — plan-review §8 (Opus subagent, re-review round). Operators commonly pull `jsonschema` in via unrelated dependencies (FastAPI, openapi-core, jsonschema-rs adapters); if strict activated whenever `jsonschema` happened to be importable, those operators would see validation behavior change on a `pip install` of an unrelated library. The explicit `validation: strict` config is the only switch.

**Exception taxonomy under strict mode** (plan-review re-round P0-2a): different failure shapes raise different exceptions so `failure_policy` can route them differently. `jsonschema.ValidationError` → `JudgeAmendedProposalRejected` (per-amendment rejection; normal `failure_policy[JudgeAmendedProposalRejected]` flow; carries the failing field path). `jsonschema.SchemaError` / `referencing.exceptions.Unresolvable` (legacy `jsonschema.RefResolutionError`) → `JudgePolicyInvalid` (operator authoring bug — the tool's own `input_schema` is malformed or has broken `$ref`s; routes through `failure_policy[JudgePolicyInvalid]`, default `block`). `ImportError` / `AttributeError` / `TypeError` from `jsonschema.validate` → `JudgeAmendedProposalRejected` with descriptive runtime-failure message. Empty (`{}`) and absent schemas are no-ops — `jsonschema.validate(args, {})` matches any object by definition, so the framework short-circuits.

**Cascade-floor strictness on `validation`** (plan-review re-round P0-1a, mirrors `class_policy`): a delegate's `judges.md` may strengthen `validation` (e.g., floor=`weakened`, delegate=`strict`) but cannot relax it. Relax attempts raise `JudgePolicyInvalid` at agent-load. The check distinguishes "delegate explicitly set `validation`" from "delegate omitted it; parser default-filled to `weakened`" via a new `JudgesConfig.validation_source` field paralleling `ClassPolicySnapshot.source` — a delegate that omits `validation:` inherits the floor's value without tripping a false-positive relax violation. Strictness ordering: `strict (2) > weakened (1)`.

**Reserved namespaces** (plan-review re-round P1-6a): `validation: audit` and `validation: paranoid` are rejected with distinct "not yet implemented" messages pointing at tracking issues ([176](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/176) and [#179](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/179) respectively). Operator typos on other values get the generic "must be one of {weakened, strict}" rejection — the two-tier UX surfaces reserved-but-not-implemented as materially different from typos.

**Per-agent warning dedup hardening** (plan-review re-round P1-5a): the module-level `_jsonschema_warned_agents` set that backs the one-shot weakened-mode warning was a known flake source. New `tests/conftest.py` autouse fixture clears the set before each test, making the "first call warns" assertion deterministic regardless of test ordering.

**Docs match reality** (CLAUDE.md taste rule 13; plan-review re-round P1-7a): `docs/spec/28-judge-layer.md` line 267's aspirational "amended tool_arguments validates against the (possibly new) tool's registered JSON schema" claim updated to point at the new `validation: strict` mode and the `[validation]` extra. Line 1024's "deferred to PR 5" note updated to point at the shipped subsection. New ` Schema validation` subsection in §"Revise" documents the modes, load-time gate, exception taxonomy, cascade-floor strictness, reserved-namespace policy, and migration aid. `docs/deployment/judges-md.md` gains a ` Validation` subsection under ` Other knobs` plus a parser-rejects list entry.

**CI matrix updated** to install the `[validation]` extra so strict-mode tests in `test_judge_revise_jsonschema.py` and the strict end-to-end tests in `test_agent_judge_revise_dispatch.py` exercise the real `jsonschema` dependency. The plan called for a third "negative-path" job that would install WITHOUT the extra and assert `JudgePolicyInvalid` at agent construction against a real Python interpreter, but that job's premise was unreachable in this dependency tree: `jsonschema` is a transitive dep of `mcp>=1.0.0` (which `atomic_agents` requires unconditionally), so a real environment without `jsonschema` would also lack `mcp` and crash the test script before reaching the parser. Coverage of the gate's behavior lives in `tests/test_judges_md_parser.py::TestValidationField::test_strict_raises_when_jsonschema_missing` via monkeypatched probe — the realistic shape of the gate in this ecosystem. The workflow file carries an explanatory comment for future maintainers; if `mcp` drops its `jsonschema` dep, the real-interpreter negative-path job can be re-added.

+28 tests pin the changes. Parser tests (`tests/test_judges_md_parser.py`): default-fill to weakened, explicit weakened/strict parse, monkeypatched import-failure raises with install instructions, audit + paranoid rejected with namespace pointers, unknown values rejected with allowed-set message, non-string rejected, case normalization, four floor-strictness scenarios (strict-vs-weakened, strict-vs-strict, omitted-inherits-floor, weakened-floor-strict-delegate-strengthens). New file `tests/test_judge_revise_jsonschema.py`: strict happy path, missing required field carries field-path, wrong type rejected, empty schema no-op, None schema no-op, malformed schema → JudgePolicyInvalid, broken $ref → JudgePolicyInvalid, weakened mode lets schema-failing amendments through, one-shot warning per agent_name dedup, default mode is weakened. End-to-end dispatch tests (`tests/test_agent_judge_revise_dispatch.py`): strict + failing amendment → `revise_invalid_amendment` BLOCK with `failed jsonschema validation` in the audit reason, strict without extra → `JudgePolicyInvalid` at agent construction (proves the load-time fail-loud gate is wired end-to-end), legacy `judges.md` without `validation:` key behaves identically to pre-PR-5b. New `tests/conftest.py` autouse fixture clearing `_jsonschema_warned_agents` keeps the warning-dedup tests deterministic.

**Closes the 112-arc-with-amendments.** The judge layer (Protocol, two reference backends, ESCALATE + REVISE state machines, `judges.md` config + cascade-aware floor, operator-driven resolution, per-class `fallback_on_timeout`, strict JSON-Schema validation) is fully shipped. Follow-up issues filed during the PR 5b arc: [175](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/175) (`check_tool_schemas_for_amendment_validation` doctor check — migration aid for `validation: strict` flip), [#176](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/176) (`validation: audit` mode), [#177](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/177) (`revise_invalid_reason` audit sub-reason), [#179](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/179) (`validation: paranoid` mode — reserved namespace; defensive `additionalProperties: false`).

- **Per-class `fallback_on_timeout` in `judges.md`** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 5a of 5b; first of the two deferred items from PR 4's CHANGELOG).

The `escalation.fallback_on_timeout` field now accepts either today's single-string form (`fallback_on_timeout: block`, applied to every `ActionClass`) OR a per-class mapping keyed by `ActionClass.value` strings. Example: `fallback_on_timeout: { default: block, high_risk: block, reversible_write: allow }`. The `default` key is REQUIRED in the dict form — there is no implicit fall-through; operators always opt into a default explicitly. Unknown class keys (operator typos) and invalid outcome values both raise `JudgePolicyInvalid` at parse time with the offending key or value named in the message.

Internally, `EscalationConfig.fallback_on_timeout` widens from `str` to `dict[str, str]`; the parser normalizes the legacy string form to `{"default": "<string>"}` so existing operator configs continue to work unchanged. `_apply_auto_decide` resolves the per-class policy using the PENDING file's frontmatter `action_class` field — the **authoritative** classification recorded at write time — NOT the on-disk directory name (Opus subagent plan-review P0: directory-name lookup would silently fall through to default on operator typos or hand-moved files, silently relaxing security-relevant policy on the class the operator most cared to protect). A `"default"`-key invariant assert at the resolution site fails loud on policies constructed without going through the parser. The on-disk ` Auto-decided by framework` block's `reason:` line now carries `fallback_on_timeout=<resolved> (resolved for action_class=<class>)` so an operator reading a stale PENDING file knows which policy fired; the `_recover_auto_decide_fallback` regex still matches the new format.

+28 tests pin the changes (16 initial + 6 coverage-audit gap-fills + 6 post-review fixes): parser tests cover legacy-string normalization, dict shape with explicit `default`, missing-`default` rejection, unknown-class-key rejection, invalid-outcome rejection, non-string/non-dict rejection, and `{revise, escalate}` rejection at parse time; auto-decide tests cover per-class override + default fall-through, frontmatter-authoritative on a typo'd directory, two files with different frontmatter in the same on-disk directory, unknown frontmatter `action_class` falling through to `default`, reason-line class recording, and the `EscalationConfig.__post_init__` invariant raising at construction. spec/28 §"Escalation queue" gains side-by-side YAML examples for both forms + a parser-rules sentence documenting both shapes, the narrowed accepted-outcome set, and the frontmatter-as-authoritative-source rule.

**Parser narrowing (/ship Step 9.1 adversarial review, cross-confirmed by 3 specialists):** accepted outcomes for `escalation.fallback_on_timeout` are now `{allow, block}` only. `revise` and `escalate` are judge-driven outcomes that require a live judge to interpret; they have no meaning in the auto-decide-when-no-judge-responded path. Pre-tightening, the parser accepted all four `JudgmentOutcome` values but `_apply_auto_decide` only branched on `allow` — `revise`/`escalate` silently coerced to `AUTO_DECIDED_BLOCK` with audit text that contradicted operator intent. The narrowed parser surfaces the gap at load time. Any `judges.md` with `fallback_on_timeout: revise` or `escalate` (legacy or per-class) now fails `JudgePolicyInvalid` at agent-load.

**Invariant moved to construction (cross-confirmed by 4 specialists):** the `"default"` key requirement for `EscalationConfig.fallback_on_timeout` moved from a runtime `assert` in `_apply_auto_decide` to `EscalationConfig.__post_init__`. The runtime guard was double-broken: `python -O` strips asserts at bytecode level, AND `AssertionError` IS-A `Exception`, so the outer `except Exception` in `poll_resolutions` would swallow it and silently wedge the PENDING file every poll cycle — exactly the opposite of the docstring's stated intent. Construction-time enforcement means violations fail loud at agent-load (parser + direct dataclass construction both surface here) and the runtime path is safe-by-construction.

Known cascade-floor gap (widened scope at [173](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/173) after /ship Step 9.1 review): cascade-floor strictness covers `class_policy` but NOT `escalation.fallback_on_timeout` (delegate may override floor's per-class timeout policy), NOT `failure_policy` (delegate may override floor's per-class-per-exception fallback), and a delegate that simply OMITS the `escalation:` section silently replaces the project floor's escalation config with parser defaults rather than inheriting the floor's. Documented in `docs/deployment/judges-md.md` under "Cascade-floor scope".

Plan-review-driven split: the second deferred item (full JSON-Schema amendment validation via an opt-in `[validation]` extra) ships in PR 5b — separated because its risk profile is "behavior change in validation" vs PR 5a's "additive config widening", and because the opt-in gate for jsonschema needs to be an explicit framework signal (env var or `judges.md` field) rather than transitive import availability (plan-review §8: operators with `jsonschema` pulled in by an unrelated dependency would otherwise see strict validation kick in without opting in via the extra).
- **JudgeBackend Protocol — spec/28 locked + conformance suite shipped** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 4 of 4, closes the arc). `tests/test_judge_protocol_conformance.py` (NEW, ~37 tests + 2 LLM-only skips on PolicyJudge) is the conformance gate that locks spec/28. Parametrizes over `PolicyJudge` (rule engine, offline) and `LLMJudgeBackend` wired to a deterministic `_StubLLMBackend` (offline; no network keys required for CI). Coverage: framework-side invariants (hash determinism + sensitivity, project-floor non-relaxable, atomic-snapshot semantics, ESCALATE state machine primitives, REVISE state machine primitives) + per-backend invariants (Protocol surface, `evaluate` idempotency, `policy_version` change-on-source-change + non-`unimplemented` sentinel, `judge_id` stability, `close()` idempotence, `supports_read_audit` + `supports_specialist_composition` bool returns, canonical `supported_outcomes` sets) + LLM-only invariants (timeout-bounded → `JudgeUnavailable`, concurrent-call connection-state integrity, UUID-canary assertion that `JudgeRuntimeConfig` fields never appear in the serialized LLM prompt). spec/28 is now **locked** — the RFC banner is dropped, per-PR-lock-in markers (PR 3a/3b/3c) folded into canonical text, escalation_queue_id contract documented as `list[str]` (multi-tool_use turns), and the full 18-value enforcement_action enum enumerated. Source-tree cleanup strips `flagged for PR 4 lock-in` scaffolding comments from `backend.py`, `types.py`, `_revise.py`, `escalation.py`, `agent.py`. `LLMJudgeBackend.supported_outcomes` widens to `{ALLOW, BLOCK, REVISE, ESCALATE}` (PR 2b's self-map to BLOCK is removed — the LLM judge is now first-class within the REVISE + ESCALATE state machines shipped in PR 3b/3c); the `judgment` tool schema gains an optional `amendment` object that the parser coerces into a `ProposalAmendment` on REVISE outcomes; `parse_judgment_tool_use` raises `JudgeUnavailable` on REVISE without amendment (fail-closed per spec/28 §"Outcome-fallback contract"). The PR 2b `revise_intent_not_supported` / `escalate_intent_not_supported` audit sentinels are removed (pre-PR-4 audit records preserve them). Closes #112. Follow-ups: per-class `fallback_on_timeout` map ships in PR 5a (above); full JSON-Schema amendment validation ships in PR 5b.
- **REVISE state machine — judge-driven amendments + operator-driven resolution** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 3c of 4). Replaces PR 3b's safety choice (Revised blocks treated as Denied) with the real REVISE flow. Two cycles ship: (1) **judge-driven REVISE** — an ensemble judge returns `Judgment(outcome=REVISE, amendment=ProposalAmendment(...))`; the framework builds an amended proposal via the new `atomic_agents/judge/_revise.py` module (`amend_proposal` recomputes classification + hashes + proposal_id from the new tool_name, carries `reason` + `authorization` verbatim per spec/28:271, appends evidence non-destructively), re-validates (`validate_amended_args` checks tool-registered + dict shape + canonical args_hash; full JSON-Schema validation deferred to PR 5b via opt-in `[validation]` extra — one-shot per-agent warning emitted), re-runs write-path enforcement, and recurses via a new factored `_run_ensemble` method to run the second judgment against the amended proposal. Bounded at `max_revise_iterations=1` per spec/28:276 — second judgment returning REVISE produces `revise_loop_exhausted_blocked`. (2) **operator-driven REVISE** — operator writes `# Revised by <op>` to a PENDING file with an embedded `amendment:` YAML block; the framework's strict parser extracts the YAML, builds the amended proposal, and **gates on the recomputed classification** (Codex round-1 P1-4 — NOT the original; otherwise an operator can swap tool_name to upgrade `reversible_write` → `high_risk` and skip the re-judge). For `high_risk` amended class: fresh ensemble re-judgment via `_run_ensemble`; only execute on ALLOW. For non-`high_risk`: schema/policy validation alone is sufficient; execute on success. Class non-downgrade by exploit (spec/28:273) is enforced in `amend_proposal` by checking the registered ToolDefinition's classification first, then tools.md, then defaulting safe. New audit values land in spec/28: `revise_pending_second_judgment`, `revise_invalid_amendment`, `revise_loop_exhausted_blocked`, `operator_revise_executed`, `operator_revise_invalid_amendment`. `JudgmentEvent` gains `revise_iteration: int` and `original_proposal_id: str | None` fields for forensic linkage. The `_dispatch_with_judge` method is factored into a thin entry point + `_run_ensemble` helper so the REVISE branch recurses against an amended proposal with a fresh JudgmentContext (CLASS NON-DOWNGRADE defense — class-policy short-circuits fire on the AMENDED classification, including BYPASS / ALLOW_WITH_AUDIT / ESCALATE). 26 new tests across 3 new files + 1 modified covering: amend_proposal field-merging + class-recomputation + class-upgrade; validate_amended_args (unknown tool, non-dict args, args_hash mismatch); enforce_amended_write_paths (read-only violation, write-path allowlist); parse_operator_amendment (happy + malformed YAML + unknown fields); judge-driven REVISE happy path (REVISE → second-judgment ALLOW → execute); second-judgment BLOCK refuses; double-REVISE → revise_loop_exhausted_blocked; REVISE with no amendment refused; unknown-tool amendment refused; operator-revise non-high_risk skips re-judge + executes; operator-revise audit re_judged=False; operator-revise high_risk re-judges via fresh ensemble + BLOCK refuses execution; operator class-upgrade via tool_name swap triggers re-judge (Codex round-1 P1-4 regression); operator-revise with no amendment block → invalid_amendment.
- **ESCALATE state machine + operator resolution polling** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 3b of 4). Replaces PR 3a's `escalate_pending_polling_unimplemented` placeholder with a real ESCALATE outcome path. When the judge ensemble returns ESCALATE — or when `class_policy.<X>=escalate` synthesizes the outcome at the framework layer — the new `atomic_agents/judge/escalation.py` module writes a PENDING file to `<agent_root>/vault/escalations/<action_class>/<proposal_id>.md` (atomic write, frontmatter + full ActionProposal serialized as fenced YAML for later integrity verification) and `agent.call()` returns `Response(deferred=True, escalation_queue_ids=[...])` so the caller sees the run paused. On a subsequent `agent.call()` (or via an explicit `agent.poll_escalations()`), the framework throttle-checks (default 60s, configurable via `escalation.resolution_poll_cycle_seconds`), scans the queue, and processes operator-resolution blocks: `# Approved by <op>` re-verifies `tool_definition_hash` against the current registry and executes the bound action inline (`cost_source="actor"`, audited as `approved_executed`); ` Denied by <op>` records refusal; ` Redacted by <op>` records redaction; ` Auto-decided by framework` covers the `auto_decide_after_seconds`-elapsed branch using `fallback_on_timeout` (default `block`). Concurrent pollers race a `.<proposal_id>.resolved-emitted` sidecar via `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` for exactly-once emit; auto-decide writes go through a sha256 compare-and-swap to defer to operator edits-in-progress. Strict resolution-block parser (h3 + exact-case verb + `by ` + operator name) refuses typos and leaves them for doctor warnings rather than silently treating as denied. Body-integrity check rehashes the ` Proposal` block's `arguments_hash` on every resolution and emits `proposal_body_tampered` audit on mismatch (defense against operators editing the proposal mid-flight). New enforcement_action values land in the spec/28 enum (`approved_executed`, `approved_stale_tool_definition`, `denied`, `redacted`, `auto_decided_block`, `auto_decided_allow`, `proposal_body_tampered`), plus `synthesis_source` (`"class_policy"` | `"failure_policy"` | null) and `triggered_by` audit fields so operators can distinguish judge-driven escalations from framework-synthesized ones — spec/28 patched inline this PR per CLAUDE.md rule 13. `Response` gains `deferred: bool` + `escalation_queue_ids: list[str]` fields (defaulted, non-breaking). `PolicyJudge.supported_outcomes()` widens to include ESCALATE; the rule engine now returns ESCALATE for `class_policy=escalate` instead of self-mapping to BLOCK. REVISE flow (judge-driven amendment + second-judgment cycle, operator ` Revised by <op>` resolution path) is deferred to PR 3c — if a Revised block appears in PR 3b it is treated as Denied (no execution against an unvalidated amendment). +35 tests across 4 new test files + modified `test_agent_judge_dispatch.py` covering writer file shape, atomic write, path traversal refusal, poller throttle, sidecar O_EXCL de-dup, first-block-wins, body integrity, strict parser, auto-decide CAS race, response deferred shape, end-to-end dispatch → poll → execute, and stale-tool-definition refusal.
- **`judges.md` operator config parser** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 3a of 4). Operators now author `judges.md` in the agent root to configure the judge layer instead of accepting PR 2a/2b's hardcoded `JUDGE_REQUIRED` default for every class. The new `atomic_agents/judges_md.py` parser reads embedded-YAML blocks (matching the `cost_guardrails` precedent in `model.md`) and produces a typed `JudgesConfig` covering: per-class policy (`bypass | allow_with_audit | judge_required | escalate` with spec/28 default-fill), per-class-per-exception `failure_policy` (accepts flat `{exception: outcome}` operator-friendly shape OR nested `{class: {exception: outcome}}` advanced shape), default judge backend + model + timeout, budget caps (daily/monthly/per-action USD), escalation destination + auto-decide-after + fallback-on-timeout, `judge_captures` + `read_audit_mode` toggles, and specialist composition axes (parsed-but-unused until PR 3b/4 ensemble configuration). Cascade-aware: in multi-agent project layouts, `<project>/judges.md` is the **non-relaxable floor** per spec/28:408 — a delegate's own `judges.md` may strengthen class policy but cannot relax it; relax attempts raise `JudgePolicyInvalid` at load. `agent.py`'s `_dispatch_with_judge` now consults parsed `class_policy` + `failure_policy` instead of the hardcoded defaults when `judges.md` is present. **No behavior change for deployments without judges.md** — the parser returns `None`, the opt-in gate from PR 2a still applies. Parser fails LOUD at agent-load time (`JudgePolicyInvalid` with actionable error messages naming the offending field + allowed values) rather than silently degrading. ESCALATE state machine + polling loop + execution back-flow ship in PR 3b; full conformance suite + spec/28 lock-in in PR 4.
- **LLM-backed judge + ensemble dispatch** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 2b of 4). The `LLMJudgeBackend` reference implementation ships today and composes the `LLMBackend` Protocol (#87) via composition. Default model `gpt-5-nano` (OpenAI; correlated-judgment mitigation against the default Anthropic actor per spec/28). System prompt assembled from `JudgePolicyContext` ONLY — the prompt-builder's parameter type is `JudgePolicyContext` (not `JudgmentContext`), so runtime config fields cannot reach the LLM judge's prompt by construction. `agent.call()`'s `_dispatch_with_judge` now invokes a two-judge ensemble: `PolicyJudge` first (microseconds, free), then `LLMJudgeBackend` if the OpenAI key resolves; first BLOCK short-circuits remaining judges (no cost incurred for downstream judges). Each judge's decision gets its own JSONL `JudgmentEvent` audit line. Tool schema accepts all four outcome values (`allow|block|revise|escalate`) so the LLM can signal revise/escalate intent in audit logs; `supported_outcomes()` advertises `{ALLOW, BLOCK}` only in PR 2b and `revise`/`escalate` self-map to BLOCK with the spec-required `revise_intent_not_supported` / `escalate_intent_not_supported` reason prefixes. Framework-side timeout wrapper (default 5s) converts timeout to `JudgeUnavailable` → `failure_policy` resolves. Determinism: `temperature=0` always passed to the wrapped LLMBackend. Lazy registration: `make_default_llm_judge` returns `None` when the OpenAI key isn't resolvable (Claude-only deployments see PolicyJudge only — no spurious `JudgeUnavailable` blocks). `policy_version` computation centralized in `judge.proposal.compute_policy_version` so both PolicyJudge and LLMJudgeBackend produce the same hash for the same `(tools.md, judges.md)` snapshot. **No behavior change for deployments that don't opt in** (opt-in gate from PR 2a still applies — `judges.md` presence or `AGENT_JUDGE_ENABLED=1`).
- **Judge dispatch + rule-engine baseline** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 2a of 4). Side-effectful tool calls now flow through a pre-action judge layer when the agent opts in (one of: `judges.md` file in the agent root, or `AGENT_JUDGE_ENABLED=1` env var). Three new framework surfaces ship: (a) the `atomic_action` side-channel marker tool — actor emits it in the same turn as any side-effectful tool call to attach reason, evidence, authorization, expected_consequence, reversibility, and target_audience to the proposal; (b) framework-side `assemble_proposal()` builds an `ActionProposal` with TOCTOU-defense hashes (`tool_definition_hash`, `arguments_hash` via `_canonical.canonical_sha256`); (c) `PolicyJudge` rule-engine reference implementation reuses `_capture.enforce_write_path` for parity with handler-side write-path enforcement, applies class policy from `ClassPolicySnapshot`, and returns ALLOW/BLOCK only (ESCALATE self-maps to BLOCK with `escalate_pending_polling_unimplemented` until the operator-resolution polling loop ships in PR 3 — avoids orphan PENDING files). `tools.md` gains a `# Tool classification` section parser mapping per-tool `ActionClass` values, and `ToolDefinition` gains a `classification` field for code-defined tools. Every judgment writes a JSONL `JudgmentEvent` audit line carrying `raw_outcome`, `enforcement_action`, `binding`, and `cost_source="judge"` per spec/28's audit shape. **Backward compatible by default** — existing v0.13.0 deployments upgrading see today's behavior unchanged unless they opt in. PR 2b adds `LLMJudgeBackend` (the LLM-backed second-line judge); PR 3 adds the `judges.md` parser and ESCALATE polling; PR 4 ships the full conformance suite and locks spec/28.
- **`JudgeBackend` Protocol scaffolding** ([112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112) — judge layer arc PR 1 of 4). The `JudgeBackend` Protocol contract, canonical dataclasses (`ActionProposal`, `Judgment`, `JudgmentEvent`, `JudgmentContext`, plus supporting policy/context/audit types), exception taxonomy (`JudgeError` + 6 subclasses), `atomic_agents/_canonical.py` JSON canonicalization helper for stable cross-process hashing, and registry primitives ship today under `atomic_agents.judge`. **No behavior change** — reference implementations (`PolicyJudge`, `LLMJudgeBackend`), `judges.md` parser, and `agent.call()` wiring follow in PRs 2-4. Existing deployments see no judge invocation until `judges.md` is added; the judge layer is opt-in per [spec/28](docs/spec/28-judge-layer.md). New top-level exports: `JudgeBackend`, `Judgment`, `JudgmentOutcome`, `JudgmentContext`, `ActionProposal`, `ProposalAmendment`, `ActionClass`, `register_judge_backend`, `get_judge_backend`, `JudgeError` (+ subclasses).
- **Project-local `/ship` adaptation** — `.claude/skills/review/checklist.md` (atomic-agents-shaped Pre-Landing Review checklist drawn from the 14 taste rules in `CLAUDE.md`) + `docs/deployment/release-runbook.md` (release runbook, two-mode workflow PR-level vs. release cut, operator manual surface check to cover what `/document-release` misses, gstack patch reapply procedure). Pairs with a local-only gstack template patch (`fix/ship-no-version-fallback` branch in `~/.claude/skills/gstack/`) that adds a `NO_VERSION_FILE` mode to `/ship` Step 12 and a missing-checklist fallback to Step 9. Closes [155](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/155).

- **CI workflow installs the `[openai]` extra.** `.github/workflows/test.yml`'s `uv sync` now installs both `dev` and `openai` extras, so the OpenAI-compatible + Moonshot backend tests added in v0.13.0 stop hitting `openai SDK not installed` failures. Pre-existing red on `main` since the v0.13.0 backend additions; surfaced by `/ship` on PR 159 and fixed inline.

0.13.0

This release closes the LLMBackend Protocol arc ([87](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/87)) — the framework now has two backend protocols in production (Memory + LLM) and three reference LLM backends (Anthropic, OpenAI direct, Moonshot) registered at framework import. Drop-in upgrade: existing agents and `model.md` configs keep working unchanged; ambiguous registrations get a new `provider:` field for disambiguation.

**Operators upgrading from v0.12.0 — three behavior changes worth knowing:**

- **Moonshot `MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` is now read once at backend construction** (was per-call). Set the env var before importing `atomic_agents` or restart the process to pick up a change.
- **Anthropic tool errors now propagate `is_error: True` on tool_result blocks** — a real improvement (the model gets a proper API-level error signal) but a new wire-shape that downstream eval harnesses comparing transcripts before/after the migration will see.
- **`format_tool_results` Protocol signature changed** from single-arg (PR 1) to three-arg (`tool_uses` + `tool_results` + `assistant_text`, PR 2). No external consumers existed when this changed; surfaced only for completeness.

Added

- **`atomic-agents review --backend kimi` subcommand ([134](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/134)) — cross-family adversarial code review via Moonshot.** Closes the gap that surfaced during PR #117 + 118 spec reviews when Codex hung mid-round and the only fallback was an Opus subagent (same model family as the author). New `atomic_agents/review.py` module with a `ReviewRequest` / `ReviewResult` dataclass surface and a built-in system prompt that enforces CLAUDE.md rule 12 (verify before claim — every finding must quote file:line evidence). CLI accepts `--prompt` or `--prompt-file`, optional `--target` (primary file under review), `--read-files` (comma-separated grounding context), `--working-dir`, `--model`, `--max-tokens` (default 16000 — reasoning-style Moonshot models like Kimi K2.x consume a large slice for internal `reasoning_content`). All operator-supplied paths flow through the framework's canonical `_io.safe_resolve_under` guard — `..` segments and absolute paths that escape `--working-dir` raise `PathTraversalError` (matches the discipline used everywhere else paths cross a trust boundary). Empty/whitespace-only `--prompt` and `--prompt-file` are rejected with exit 1 before the LLM call (no silent paid no-op). When a reviewer returns empty visible content (the documented K2.x thinking-model failure mode — see [146](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/146)), a WARNING precedes the cost summary so operators don't pay for blank reviews silently. Review output writes to stdout; cost summary to stderr so piping to a file doesn't pollute the artifact. Default model is `moonshot/moonshot-v1-128k` (non-thinking, produces visible content reliably); Kimi K2.6 / K2.5 are priced in the table but require [#146](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/146)'s reasoning_content extraction work before they become the recommended reviewer. 26 unit tests covering prompt assembly, path-traversal refusal (relative, absolute, target + read-files), file resolution, backend dispatch, model overrides, cost summary stream routing, empty-content warning, empty-prompt guards, and CLI integration. Live-validated against open PR #145 ($0.005 / 21s per review). `docs/methodology.md` §"Codex as a real outside voice" gains a new "Reviewer roster" sub-section explaining when to use Codex vs Opus subagent vs Kimi, including the honest empirical note that today's Kimi default model is a weaker reviewer than Opus or Codex (use as third opinion alongside, not as substitute) and a security caveat that `MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` determines where the API key + prompt + read-files contents are sent.

- **`_llm._call_moonshot` reads `MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` / `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` env vars for endpoint override.** Operators with keys issued via the international portal (`api.moonshot.ai`) can now use the framework; default behavior unchanged (`api.moonshot.cn`). Stopgap until proper per-region routing lands with the LLMBackend protocol ([87](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/87)).

- **`_costs.PRICING` extended with Moonshot model entries.** `moonshot/moonshot-v1-{8k,32k,128k}` (non-thinking), `moonshot/kimi-k2.6` + `moonshot/kimi-k2.5` (thinking, api.moonshot.ai naming), and `moonshot/kimi-k2-0905-preview` + `moonshot/kimi-k2-0711-preview` (thinking, api.moonshot.cn naming — also referenced by the existing `tests/test_llm_tool_uses.py` fixture). All entries at placeholder rates of $0.30 / $1.20 per Mtok in/out; verify against Moonshot's current published pricing before depending on dashboard cost totals.

- **README hero diagram** — at-a-glance SVG at the top of the README showing the three core value claims (agent-as-folder, stateless cost-capped runtime, grepable JSONL audit trail) and the four shipped runtime shapes (`cron` · `launchd` · Claude Code skill · embedded Python). Light + dark variants wired via `<picture>` element so each viewer's system color scheme is matched. Source SVGs at `docs/assets/atomic-agents-hero.svg` and `docs/assets/atomic-agents-hero-dark.svg`. Every in-diagram claim — `Response` field names, JSONL top-level log shape, the runtime list — verified against shipped code (`atomic_agents/types.py` for the dataclass surface, `docs/samples/caldwell/log/` for real log shape, `extras/` for the runtime ports) per CLAUDE.md taste rules 12 + 13; an Opus subagent stood in for the rate-limited Codex round and caught four drift claims (invented `run_id`/`captures` JSONL fields, mistyped `Response(text, cost, run_id)`, aspirational `MCP server`/`HTTP service` runtime chips, drifted "21 locked spec docs" count) before commit.

- **`docs/spec/30-responsibility-audit.md`** — design spec for the responsibility audit primitive: a scheduled or on-demand offline-reflection surface that reads cross-cutting state (tools.md + judges.md + mandates.md + recent run logs + escalation queue) and produces a structured per-action-class coverage report with gap analysis as the primary output. Status: RFC. Origin: [116](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/116). Implementation pending in follow-up issues filed after spec merges. Defines the six-row coverage model (Discovery / Authorization / Action execution / Evidence / Reversibility / Escalation) generalized from commerce; per-agent vs project-level scope; CLI surface (`atomic-agents audit responsibility`) with on-demand + scheduled + doctor-triggered modes; audit-output file format and frontmatter schema; rule-engine vs LLM enrichment two-mode operation; new `cost_source: "audit"` ledger value (sibling of `actor` and `judge`); 4 audit event types (audit_started / audit_completed / audit_failed / audit_budget_exhausted); composition rules with the eval framework (sibling, not collapsed), the dream pipeline (sibling shape, different layer), the doctor (bidirectional cross-reference), and the future PolicyBackend (#89) (fleet-scale composition); 6 doctor checks (check_responsibility_audit_age, check_responsibility_audit_gap_count, check_responsibility_audit_stale_policy, check_responsibility_audit_unused_mandates, check_responsibility_audit_escalation_drift, check_audit_budget_exhausted); backward compat as opt-in (audit never runs automatically; operators schedule explicitly); 5 open questions documented for impl-PR resolution.

- **`docs/spec/29-mandates.md`** — design spec for the mandate primitive: durable, operator-granted scoped authority records that live in `mandates.md`, are referenced by side-effectful action proposals via `mandate_id`, and validated by the judge layer's new `MandateCheck` specialist. Status: RFC. Origin: [115](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/115). Implementation pending in follow-up issues filed after spec merges. Defines the `Mandate` + `MandateConstraints` + `TargetPattern` + `TimeWindow` dataclasses; the `mandates.md` file format with parser rules; per-agent vs project-root resolution (with can-only-tighten discipline mirroring spec/28's judge-policy floors); the `Authorization` shape extension (`granted_by: "mandate:<id>"` + `mandate_id` field); the `MandateCheck` judge specialist's 8-step validation order; cost-event ledger split (`cost_source: "mandate:<id>"`) with reservation pattern for concurrent-action TOCTOU defense; 7 mandate lifecycle event shapes; `judges.md`'s new `# Mandates` configuration section; 5 doctor checks (`check_mandate_health`, `check_mandate_no_expiry`, `check_mandate_id_collisions`, `check_mandate_relaxation_violations`, `check_mandate_source_hash_drift`); read-only CLI surface (`atomic-agents mandate list / show / usage` — no `grant`/`revoke` subcommands because the file IS the operator's grant); backward-compat as opt-in.

- **`docs/spec/01-anatomy.md` §"Graduated autonomy"** — new framework-level section naming graduated autonomy as a stated property of the framework, with the four-class action taxonomy table from spec/28 and the principle that the same agent definition runs at every scale by configuring `tools.md` / `judges.md` / `mandates.md` rather than re-shaping the agent. `docs/spec/28-judge-layer.md` Overview gains a one-paragraph cross-link to the new section, framing the judge layer as the mechanism that encodes the principle.

- **`docs/spec/28-judge-layer.md`** — design spec for the judge layer: a pre-action validation surface that gates side-effectful tool calls behind a `JudgeBackend` returning one of four outcomes (allow / block / revise / escalate). Defines action proposal schema, four-outcome model, `JudgeBackend` protocol surface + capability advertisement, default `LLMJudgeBackend` reference implementation, action classification (read-only / reversible-write / external-side-effect / high-risk), specialist judge composition pattern, memory provenance label extension (observed / inferred / generated / confirmed / disputed / superseded), `judges.md` operator config aesthetic, cost treatment per design rule 4, JSONL judgment-event audit shape per design rule 5, eval-suite shape for judges, five named failure modes with mitigations (correlated judgment / specification gaming / escalation drift / latency-cost / policy drift), backward-compat opt-in stance per design rule 14, and five open questions to resolve at spec lock. Status `planned`. Origin: RFC [110](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/110). Implementation pending in follow-up issues filed after spec merges.

Changed

- **`_costs.sum_cost_for_period()` — optional `source` + `mandate_id` filters ([122](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/122)).** Two new keyword-only parameters: `source` (`"actor"` / `"judge"` / `"audit"` / `None`) filters cost events by their origin per spec/28's actor/judge ledger split and spec/30's audit-cost extension; `mandate_id` filters by the authorizing mandate per spec/29. Filters AND together; both default to `None` (legacy behavior — sum every record). Cost-event JSONL records now carry `cost_source: "actor"` explicitly; legacy records without the field are treated as actor spend on read so existing logs continue to read correctly. The 8 existing actor cost-guardrail callers (6 in `agent.py`, 2 in `dream.py`) now pass `source="actor"` defensively — zero behavior change today, but ensures the actor cap won't double-count judge or audit spend when the judge ([#112](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/112)) and audit ([#125](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/125)) runtimes start writing to the same log files. Foundation for spec/28 judge ledger, spec/29 mandate budgets, and spec/30 audit ledger. **Known gap:** `atomic_agents/outcome.py:589-606` and `eval.py:710-713` still write `agent_cost_usd` / `judge_cost_usd` records with a parallel schema that the new filter doesn't see — tracked in [#144](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/144) (Option A/B/C decision pending). 13 new unit tests covering each filter combination, legacy-record compat, multi-source aggregation, and pinned behavior on unrecognized `cost_source` values. No breaking changes — all existing call sites and log files continue to work unchanged.

- **`CLAUDE.md` §"What we're explicitly NOT building"** — added Graphify-style knowledge-graph integration to the list of explicitly-deferred ideas, with rationale (INDEX-driven recall is the load-bearing memory pattern per spec/02 + spec/04; graphify's "visualize the whole graph" aesthetic is the opposite; substrate mismatch — graphify's strength is code semantics, not YAML + JSONL). Mirrors the canonical entry in `~/ObsidianVault/Atomic Agents/ROADMAP.md` §"What I'd NOT pursue". Three lightweight in-framework alternatives filed as separate issues ([138](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/138) goal timeline, [#139](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/139) delegation cost treemap, [#140](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/140) spec cross-reference Mermaid diagram).

Process notes (operator-visible context, not behavior changes)

- **Methodology drift recovery.** PRs [145](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/145), [#147](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/147), [#149](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/149), [#151](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/151), [#152](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/152), [#153](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/153), [#154](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/pull/154) all landed in the v0.13.0 development window via manual `git push` + `gh pr create` workflows, bypassing the `/ship` skill that CLAUDE.md flags as mandatory. This release cuts the tag + promotes `[Unreleased]` via the canonical `/ship` pipeline, recovering the discipline. [#155](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/155) (`/ship` workflow adaptation gaps for atomic-agents) tracks the work to ensure `/ship` runs end-to-end on this project — the manual fallbacks during the arc were symptoms of unfit-for-this-project skill defaults (4-digit VERSION expectation, missing review checklist, gstack-bin dependencies). User-level `feedback_always_ship.md` memory strengthened in the same session to prevent recurrence.

Follow-up implementation issues filed after spec lock: `JudgeBackend` protocol + filesystem-default reference implementation; memory-provenance frontmatter extension; reference judge wired to `tools.md` enforcement.
- **`[backend] LLMBackend Protocol — closed (87): canonical types + 3 reference backends + registry + spec/31 + conformance suite`** ([87](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/87)) — establishing the LLM provider abstraction layer in the protocol-pattern series alongside #60–65 / 82–84. **PR 1** shipped scaffolding (canonical types, `SyncLLMBackend` Protocol, registry primitives) under the same `[Unreleased]` entry. **PR 2** lands the first concrete backend and routes Claude models through it: new `atomic_agents/llm/anthropic.py` with `AnthropicLLMBackend` implementing all 7 Protocol methods (provider_id, supports_model, capabilities per family with conservative defaults for forward-compat against future Claude releases, pricing delegating to `_costs.PRICING`, count_tokens via SDK with heuristic fallback, call wrapping the SDK with canonical-tools translation, format_tool_results building Anthropic's assistant-echo + user-tool_result message sequence). Lazy default registration in `atomic_agents/llm/__init__.py` — backends register on first `find_backend_for_model` call rather than at import time so subprocess spawn (e.g. `multiprocessing.Process` in tests) doesn't pay anthropic-import latency. `_llm.call_llm` for `claude-*` models now dispatches through `find_backend_for_model(model).call(...)`; `_call_anthropic` deleted (dead code after the routing change). Legacy dict-shape tools converted to canonical at the entry boundary (per-element shape check) so external callers passing Anthropic-shape dicts continue to work; legacy openai-shape preserved by reference when every item is a dict (preserves test object-identity assertions for the OpenAI/Moonshot path that still flows through procedural dispatch). Protocol's `format_tool_results` signature extended from single-arg to three-arg (`tool_uses` + `tool_results` + `assistant_text`) — Anthropic's tool loop needs all three to build the assistant-echo turn. New `_capture.canonical_tool_definition()` + `ToolRegistry.to_canonical_definitions()` helpers in place ready for the PR 2.5 `agent.py` tool-dispatch refactor (descoped from PR 2 after the bundled attempt triggered 25 test failures across the suite). 26 new unit tests for AnthropicLLMBackend (`test_llm_anthropic_backend.py`) covering family resolution, per-model capabilities, pricing delegation, count_tokens SDK/heuristic paths, call() text/tool_use/cache/tools-translation, format_tool_results assistant-echo + atomic_capture skip + is_error + dict-content-json-serialization, forward-compat property on unknown Claude families, and the legacy-dict cache_control drop pinned as a known gap (issue 150 tracks closing it). Opus subagent adversarial review caught 2 P1s (forward-compat regression on future Claude model ids + silent per-tool `cache_control` drop) + 3 P2s (heterogeneous-list crash, broad `except Exception` swallowing init bugs, redundant `except (AttributeError, Exception)`) + cheap P3s (docstring drift, dead-code removal) — all addressed pre-merge with verifying tests. 150 (per-tool cache_breakpoint canonical-type extension) filed inline. **PR 2.5** completes the agent.py refactor descoped from PR 2: `_capture_tool_definitions(model)` and `_all_tool_definitions(model)` now return `list[LLMToolDefinition]` regardless of provider (model.startswith branching gone from those methods); `_build_tool_loop_messages(model)` Anthropic branch routes through `AnthropicLLMBackend.format_tool_results` using a new private `_canonicalize_tool_loop` helper that PR 3 will reuse for OpenAI/Moonshot. `tools.build_tool_result_blocks_anthropic` deleted (dead code post-refactor). Wire-byte parity with pre-87 preserved: tool-result content is json-encoded with a `str()` fallback for non-JSON-serializable outputs (datetime, custom classes) so operator JSONL transcripts before/after the migration diff cleanly. Tool errors now propagate Anthropic's `is_error: true` flag on tool_result blocks (a real improvement — gives the model proper recovery signal — but a NEW behavior worth noting). Opus subagent adversarial review caught 2 P1s on wire-byte parity (string outputs no longer json-encoded, non-JSON-serializable outputs crashed) + 2 P2s (tautological assignment, missing second-iteration test coverage) + cleanups — all addressed pre-merge with 4 new tests pinning the wire-byte contract. **PR 3** completes the dispatch unification — every supported provider (Claude, GPT, Moonshot) now routes through the registry. New `atomic_agents/llm/openai_compat.py` ships `OpenAICompatibleLLMBackend`, a single configurable class with six dimensions (`provider_id`, `KeySpec`, `model_namespace`, `model_transform`, `base_url`, `capability_hooks`) per the codex P3 from the plan. `make_openai_backend()` factory configures OpenAI direct; `atomic_agents/llm/moonshot.py`'s `make_moonshot_backend()` configures Moonshot's prefix-stripping transform + region-aware `base_url` (resolved once at backend construction from `ATOMIC_AGENTS_MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` → `MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` → China endpoint default). `_llm.call_llm` collapses to a single registry dispatch; the procedural `_call_openai` / `_call_moonshot` / `_call_anthropic` (the last already gone) plus the `_extract_openai_tool_calls` helper are deleted. `_extract_openai_tool_calls` is re-exported as a thin shim for back-compat (tests and any external pinning continue to work). `agent.py._build_tool_loop_messages` collapses: no more `if model.startswith("claude-")` branch — every provider's tool-loop continuation flows through `backend.format_tool_results(...)`. `tools.build_tool_result_blocks_openai` deleted (dead code post-refactor). `model.md` gains an optional `provider:` field parsed in `_model.py`; `AgentConfig.provider` carries the preference; `agent.py` threads it as `preferred_provider=` through every `_llm.call_llm` call site AND through the tool-loop's `find_backend_for_model` lookup. The new `_to_canonical_tool_defs` adapter accepts both Anthropic-shape AND OpenAI-shape legacy dicts so any external code that pre-87 passed provider-shaped dicts to `call_llm` continues to work. **Behavior change (worth noting):** Moonshot's `MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` env var is now read once at backend construction (lazy on first `find_backend_for_model` lookup) rather than per-call. The pre-87 procedural `_call_moonshot` re-read the var on every call; operators who set the var after the first backend lookup will see the previous value. Set the var before importing atomic_agents or restart the process to pick up changes. 35 new unit tests (24 OpenAI backend + 6 Moonshot + 5 model.md parser); 4 end-to-end regression tests in `test_llm_provider_threading.py` pin the `model.md provider:` → registry threading contract (both the main LLM call AND the tool-loop continuation). Opus subagent adversarial review caught 1 P1 (missing `preferred_provider` thread to `find_backend_for_model` in `_build_tool_loop_messages` — would have crashed iteration 2 of a multi-turn tool loop with `AmbiguousBackendError` once any operator registered a second `gpt-*`-claiming backend) + 4 P2s (provider regex matching inside YAML blocks, missing end-to-end test, `MOONSHOT_BASE_URL` semantics change undocumented, Moonshot prefix-strip first-only behavior) — all addressed pre-merge with regression tests. **PR 4** closes the arc: new `docs/spec/31-llm-backend.md` (full prose contract mirroring `spec/20-memory-backend.md`'s shape — Overview, Module layout, Canonical request/response types, Protocol surface with per-method semantics, Capability advertisement, Backend registration + conflict resolution, Reserved future Protocols, Reference implementations, Tool definition / tool result translation, Wire-byte parity discipline, Exceptions, Conformance requirements, Call-site migration reference, Open questions for v2). New `tests/test_llm_protocol_conformance.py` parameterizes the suite over every registered backend (Anthropic, OpenAI direct, Moonshot) and asserts 23 conformance properties × 3 backends = 70 conformance tests covering Protocol surface, provider_id stability, supports_model side-effect-freedom, capabilities honesty, pricing fallback, count_tokens monotonicity, call() canonical-tools translation + tool_use normalization, format_tool_results contract including wire-byte parity + atomic_capture skip + is_error handling + non-JSON-serializable fallback. README's `What's shipped` table gains LLMBackend; the backend-roadmap table moves LLMBackend from "Planned" to "✅ Shipped"; CLAUDE.md's architecture diagram backend-protocols line gains `LLM ✅`; CLAUDE.md §Status updated. Total tests: 928 passing (was 858 pre-PR — +70 net new from the conformance suite). **87 is now closed.** The remaining tracked follow-ups (146 reasoning_content extraction, 148 top_p/stop_sequences parity, 150 per-tool cache_breakpoint) are tracked Protocol extensions, not v1 contract gaps. Will land across a future Minor release (additive; no ` BREAKING` callout). 148 (top_p/stop_sequences parity) + 146 (Kimi K2.x reasoning_content extraction) + 150 (per-tool cache_breakpoint) tracked as follow-ups.
- **`[deployment] Configuration wizard + deploy assistant for new operators`** ([94](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/94)) — `/atomic-init` Claude Code skill that walks new operators conversationally from "I want to build an agent" to "my agent ran its first call." Drafts plausible IDENTITY/SOUL/USER from a role description, picks defaults for `tools.md` / `model.md`, configures secrets, and prints the invocation command. Post-public-flip work.
- **`[polish] Public API surface lock — promote missing exceptions to __all__ + CLI exit-code parity`** ([99](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/99)) — 9 exception classes are raised inside the package but not advertised via `atomic_agents/__init__.py`'s `__all__`. Promote the clearly user-facing ones (`NestedDelegationRefused`, `CaptureParseError`, `GoalCorrupted`, `ToolNameCollision`, the MCP triplet). Also map CLI exit codes 1:1 to exception classes so shell scripts can react differently to different failure classes.
- **`[deployment] Add per_call_usd field to cost_guardrails YAML schema`** ([100](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/100)) — today's `cost_guardrails` supports `daily_usd` / `monthly_usd` but no per-call cap. Runaway-loop protection and predictable per-call billing both want this. New `per_call_usd` + `per_call_cap_action` fields, pre-call cost estimation, tree-cap interaction, `PerCallCapBlocked` exception in `__all__`.

0.12.0

Public-flip-readiness Minor. Documentation-only — no framework behavior changes from v0.11.0; runtime ships unchanged. Drop-in upgrade from v0.11.0 — no ` BREAKING` callouts.

This is the **launch shape** release: the README rewrite that anchors the public-flip narrative, the repo-surface kit standard for an OSS project (CONTRIBUTING / CODE_OF_CONDUCT / SECURITY / issue + PR templates), and a LICENSE consistency fix that closes the final maintainer-name drift left from the personal-references scrub. Codex adversarial review caught six factual errors / overclaims in the original README draft before merge; all six were applied.

Changed

- **README rewritten for the public flip.** Framework-first positioning replaces the prior feature-list opener. New tagline lands as the hero — *"AI agents that live in your folder, not someone else's database"* with subtitle *"Vault-native, MIT-licensed, Markdown-source-of-truth."* New `Why this exists` opener names "agent state ends up in app databases / vector stores / hosted trace systems / bespoke glue code" as the precise enemy rather than naming specific competitors as universally hosted. New `Current limits` section makes the alpha / single-maintainer / macOS-Linux-primary / only-MemoryBackend-shipped / log-only-alerts state explicit before the comparison matrix. New honest comparison matrix names Letta, Mem0, LangGraph + LangSmith, and direct-SDK with narrower defensible claims (Markdown-source-of-truth, no required server, spec-level file layout) — and a `Where the alternatives win` paragraph names where each does better than Atomic Agents. Backend-protocol scaling section now labels org-scale-over-Postgres as `v1 direction` rather than implying shipped today. 6 deployment runbooks linked. Spec count corrected (13 → 21). Caldwell description sharpened to surface the 5 days of real JSONL logs, the helper-pattern day with ~76% cost savings, and the evals across happy/edge/adversarial/decline categories. Caldwell appears as one sample among future samples, not as the headline.
- **README badge URLs corrected** from `github.com/user/*` (broken) to `github.com/dep0we/*`. Version badge added.
- **README default `ATOMIC_AGENTS_ROOT` corrected** from `~/agents/agents` (duplicated path, typo) to `~/docs/agents` per `atomic_agents/_platform.py:DEFAULT_AGENTS_ROOT`.
- **README "spec docs are not aspirational" softened** to "spec docs separate shipped behavior from explicit future/deferred boundaries" — closer to ground truth (some spec sections mark future work explicitly).
- **Status section** updated v0.10 → v0.11.0; protocol-pattern v1.0 expectation named.

Added

- **`CONTRIBUTING.md`** — reading order before opening a PR (CLAUDE.md / TENSIONS.md / methodology.md), branch + commit shape, test expectations, review-in-rounds practice, what lands cleanly vs what needs an issue first.
- **`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`** — condensed Contributor-Covenant-shaped policy with the project's actual maintainer contact and an enforcement table; not vendored boilerplate.
- **`SECURITY.md`** — 90-day disclosure window, in-scope vs documented-honest-limitations (best-effort path-traversal check in MCP args, advisory-only cost guardrails without the shared helper, plain-markdown-no-encryption-at-rest), operator hygiene checklist.
- **`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/{bug,feature,question}.md`** — structured templates matching the project's existing issue conventions (title prefixes, env/repro/scope sections).
- **`.github/pull_request_template.md`** — mirrors the project's existing PR shape (Summary / Why / Test plan / Design alignment self-check against the 14 CLAUDE.md design rules).

Fixed

- **`LICENSE` copyright line** flipped from `Copyright (c) 2026 Dan Powers` to `Copyright (c) 2026 atomic-agents-stack contributors`. Matches the `pyproject.toml` author field from the personal-references scrub (issue 77 / PR 92). The Codex adversarial review of the launch README flagged the inconsistency between LICENSE and `pyproject.toml` as small but visible drift — the only place the maintainer name still surfaced after the scrub.

Process notes (operator-visible context, not behavior changes)

- **Codex adversarial review run against the launch README before the public flip** (per the `codex_reviews_mandatory` operator preference). Six findings caught and applied: factual errors in the comparison matrix (Letta has self-hosted Docker; Mem0 OSS exists; LangGraph has filesystem-backed memory via Deep Agents and LangSmith for observability — original matrix overclaimed all four), `runs anywhere markdown does` softened to `Markdown-source-of-truth` because `atomic_agents/_locks.py` imports POSIX `fcntl` unconditionally, `spec docs are not aspirational` softened to `separate shipped behavior from explicit future/deferred boundaries`, dangling "Your first agent below" reference removed, and the LICENSE consistency fix above. Recommendation: Revise before public flip — adopted.

0.11.0

Documentation-heavy Minor focused on **public-flip readiness**. Closes the operator-doc gaps that were blockers for a credible public release: every supported deployment shape now has its own runbook, every public exception is cataloged, and the Caldwell sample is unambiguously fictional. No new framework features; the runtime ships unchanged from v0.10.0. Drop-in upgrade from v0.10.0 — no ` BREAKING` callouts.

Added

- **`docs/deployment/obsidian.md` — Obsidian-backed deployment guide** (833 lines, closes [67](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/67)). The operator runbook for running an Atomic Agent on top of an Obsidian-synced vault. Covers recommended vault layout, `.obsidian/sync.ignore` patterns with per-line rationale, `.obsidian/` config dir handling, `AgentLock` race conditions vs Sync writes, `_dashboard/index.html` self-containment, conflict copy recovery, a 9-step worked first-run example, and cross-platform read/edit-vs-run boundaries. Cites concrete code paths (`_locks.py:54-77`, `_io.atomic_write`, `_platform.py:get_agents_root`, `mcp.py:616`, `memory/filesystem.py` walker, `migrate.py:find_content_files`, `dashboard/render.py:7-9`).
- **`docs/deployment/programmatic.md` — Programmatic invocation guide + complete public exception table** (761 lines, closes [69](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/69)). The Python-embedded path for using the framework inside another app. Covers when to use programmatic vs CLI, `Agent` + `call()` public surface, cost-guardrail handling, memory API (canonical `agent.memory.*` + deprecated re-exports), helper/delegate semantics with the one-level constraint, concurrency model, three worked examples (single-shot cron embed, custom orchestrator, subprocess-safe gunicorn-shaped worker), and a complete public exception table covering 18 exported classes plus a "raised but not yet in `__all__`" subsection for 9 internal ones (follow-up tracked in [#99](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/99)).
- **`docs/deployment/disaster-recovery.md` — Disaster recovery runbook** (741 lines, closes [72](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/72)). Symptom-organized runbook for first-response when something goes wrong. Nine scenarios covering stale-lock recovery (`lsof` diagnosis, why `.lock` persists after flock release), mid-run crash recovery via atomic-write guarantees, corrupted INDEX repair via `FilesystemBackend.list_orphans()`, migration rollback flow, memory-write races, Obsidian Sync conflict recovery, `.versions/` snapshot management, doctor failure-mode mapping, and the git-as-canonical-backup pattern. Every recovery shows the exact command to run plus how to verify the fix.
- **`docs/deployment/cost-guardrail-sizing.md` — Cost guardrail sizing guidance** (521 lines, closes [73](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/73)). How to pick numbers for daily/monthly caps and cap action. Includes a current pricing-per-MTok snapshot from `_costs.py`, the 14-day observe-then-apply pattern from spec/09, and **seven role archetypes with recommended starting caps**: personal financial advisor, daily-brief cron, interactive skill-mode, helper-heavy summarizer, goal-driven autonomous, high-stakes single-call modeling, and multi-role coordinator. Names a real schema gap (no `per_call_usd` field today) that's tracked as a follow-up in [#100](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/100).
- **Cross-references between the four new deployment docs** so a reader landing on any one of them can fan out to the others without scrolling back to a directory listing.

- README "What's shipped" table refreshed to add v0.10.0 rows (MCP client, MemoryBackend, doctor, deployment docs) and consolidate the inflated `v0.2`–`v0.8` labels — those modules all actually shipped in v0.9.0, the leading-zero `v0.X` labels were aspirational milestone numbers from the build sequence. Versions in the table are now real release tags.
- README "What's shipped" table swept again post-cluster to add four new deployment-doc rows (`obsidian.md`, `programmatic.md`, `disaster-recovery.md`, `cost-guardrail-sizing.md`) and broaden the `docs/deployment/` description in the Repository structure block. The four rows ship as `✅ v0.11.0`.
- Public-API audit (filed as follow-up [99](https://github.com/dep0we/atomic-agents-stack/issues/99)) — discovered while documenting the programmatic invocation path that 9 exception classes are raised inside the package but not in `atomic_agents/__init__.py`'s `__all__`. The new `programmatic.md` documents current behavior honestly and surfaces the gap; the actual `__all__` promotion + CLI exit-code parity work is queued for a future Minor.
